# Claude Code prompt pack 2: Phases 6 to 15

Written 2026-10-13 against `main` at `1cb9cf2`, which is what https://esichak.github.io/personal-website/ serves today. This file is the one Eric pastes from. Its companion is the second improvement plan, dated 2026-10-13, which holds every finding with its evidence, the decisions list, and what the review left out and why. The prompts below carry enough of each finding to work from without the plan open; the plan is where to look when a prompt's reason is not enough.

How the findings were made: a set of reviewers read the sources at `1cb9cf2`, the local build in `_site/` made from that commit, the live site (with `curl`), the screenshots in `.cache/plan2/shots/` (desktop at 1280 px, phone at 390 px), and the earlier plan, report and log. They ran Lighthouse on the live home page and the Matterhorn report (accessibility, best practices and SEO) but took no page-load traces, so every loading claim below rests on the Phase 4 measurements in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md` sections 2 and 14.3 and on byte counts, not on new timings. Where a prompt asks for a measurement, use the method in `docs/DEVELOPING.md` 'Measuring page loads' (around line 1428).

## How to use this file

- One phase per Claude Code session, in order, Phase 6 first. Paste the Standing instructions block, then the phase's Prompt (Phase 13 is three prompts, 13A, 13B and 13C, one session each). Nothing else is needed. Where the plan's Files line names a tool the prompt leaves out, the prompt is the scope.
- Read the session's report and look at its screenshots before starting the next phase. The Gate line under each phase says what must be true before you move on.
- Phase 6 needs no decision from you; Phase 7 asks three with defaults that stand if you say nothing. Every other phase opens with the decisions it needs; answer them in the first message of the session or let the recommended default stand.
- Phase 13 is three prompts and three sessions. Phase 15 is one evening of rendering plus one session before it and one after.
- The last prompt, 'Review gate before each publication', runs before every push: after Phase 15, or after whichever phase you decide to publish from, and ends with a push only when you say so.

## Standing instructions (paste first in every session)

```
You are working in the personal-website repository, in its checkout on Eric's Mac (git; main is the published branch, and the work of a phase happens on a branch made from it). Before doing anything else, read the workspace CLAUDE.md one folder above the repository (../CLAUDE.md; from Phase 8 on, the repository's own CLAUDE.md as well), README.md, docs/DEVELOPING.md in full, and the latest entry of docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md. The design system and the hard rules are in docs/DEVELOPING.md from line 58 (Datum) and line 72 (Rules): facts only from the content files, write-ups verbatim, no invented dates, captions, grades or conditions, every distance and height through core.U / core.distance / core.elev with the MI|KM switch, relative links through core.link, static maps through core.map_block, accessibility rules, and what must work without JavaScript. Keep the tools compatible with Python 3.9 (the Mac's /usr/bin/python3 is 3.9.6; CI runs 3.12).

Working rules for this session:
1. Work on a branch phase-N-<slug> made from main (the previous phases were built on a branch and fast-forwarded onto main at publication, so main has no merge commits) and never push, merge or deploy; a push happens only when Eric says so in his own words in this session. The planned pushes are Phase 8's rollback rehearsal and dispatch test, Phase 15's render round if he publishes it without the gate, and the review gate before each publication; each of those still asks first. End the phase with one commit per phase on that branch, staged by path, message 'Phase N: <title>' (a second commit for the log entry is fine); the 'before' build the gates compare against is a build of HEAD made before any edit.
2. Stage by explicit path (git add "content/trips/<slug>" ...). Never run git add -A or git add . : the thousands of iCloud conflict copies under rendered/ and assets/ (named like 'meta 3.json' or 'map-wide 4.webp') are git-ignored and never committed, but content/ can gain an 'index 2.md' or 'days/02 2.md' copy that is not ignored and that the validator stops the build on. Read git status --short before every commit and refuse any ' 2', ' 3', ' 4' name.
3. Never run tools/render.py unless a track or a map setting changed and Eric agreed in this session (it needs the internet and rewrites committed files under rendered/); CI never renders.
4. Keep scratch copies tiny. Never copy the checkout, except the one worktree the review gate makes under the scratch folder and removes at the end. Build with: python3 tools/build.py --out <scratch folder> --link --check (symlinks, about 4 s). The scratch folder is the session's scratchpad directory (named in the system prompt under Scratchpad directory) or ~/Library/Caches/personal-website/ (the same folder that holds the render caches tiles/ and osm/ from Phase 6 and site/ for builds); never --out _site/ (240 MB of copies that stall when iCloud has offloaded rendered/). A partial build (--only) must not carry --check. Under 30 GB free (about 19 GB today), say so in the report, keep scratch builds linked, make no worktree, and ask Eric to clear space before Phase 15's render round; Phase 8's doctor.py prints the same WARN.
5. Run the tests on both Pythons with warnings as errors before the phase ends: /usr/bin/python3 -W error -m unittest discover -s tests and python3.12 -W error -m unittest discover -s tests. One module: python3 -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_x.py. The suite takes about 80 s locally; the Node harnesses run when node is on PATH.
6. Facts only from content: no new sentence about a trip, no caption, no grade, no condition, no date. Interface copy (labels, buttons, headings) is allowed and is listed in the report as copy Eric should read.
7. Previews before a visual decision: when a phase asks Eric to choose how something looks, build the options side by side (same page, same width) and show him the pictures before asking; never ask him to pick blind.
8. Privacy: the repository is public. Write nothing into code, tests, docs or the log that names a home location, start coordinates, which trips share a start, or times someone is away. docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md is the level of detail allowed. Fixtures are synthetic (tests/gpx_fixtures.py, tests/helpers.py); never a real write-up or a real recording in tests/.
9. Cite what you touch: when you change a documented rule, say so in the log entry with the file and line of the rule.
10. Write one entry in docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md at the end of the phase, in the format of the previous entries ('## <Title> (<date>)', '### Changed files' table, '### Behaviour' bullets, '### Checks actually run' table ending with what was not run, '### Remaining issues and author inputs'). The checks table names only checks that actually ran, with the build they ran on (commit or 'working tree at <time>') and the Python version. Never claim a check you did not run.

Report format at the end of the phase (in the chat, after the log entry is written):
- Changed files: one line each, what changed.
- Commit: the branch and the sha of the phase's commit (and of the log commit, if separate).
- Behavior: what a visitor or Eric sees differently, page by page.
- Tests run and their results: the two Pythons with -W error (counts, time), build.py --out <scratch> --link --check (page and trip counts), the Node harnesses, any stdlib script the gate asked for, with the numbers the gate asks for.
- Screenshots captured: file names under .cache/plan2/<phase>/ with the width and page of each.
- Problems met and how they were resolved, or left.
- Missing author inputs: decisions, text or files only Eric can supply.
- The next smallest step.
```

## Phase 6 - Author tools: safe imports, instant preview, stale-map warnings

Everything in this phase sits under `tools/` and `tests/`, with the guides that describe them. Nothing on the public site changes except one truthful empty state for a trip whose maps are not rendered. No decision from Eric is needed; the one open input (which trips to re-time) is his private list from `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md` 12.12 and the tool ships regardless.

### Prompt

```
PHASE 6: Author tools: safe imports, instant preview, stale-map warnings.

Goal: fix the traps in the tools Eric runs for every trip (a --force that erases a write-up, two front-matter parser traps, maps drawn from old inputs with no warning, half-written render folders, a 240 MB copying preview, a render cache that iCloud offloads) and make every weekly step a few seconds, all inside tools/ and tests/, with the import tools' first tests. No page changes except resilience-12's empty map box. No decision from Eric is needed before starting.

Do the items in this order. Each has the observation (what is true at 1cb9cf2, with file:line), the proposal, and the files.

First build HEAD into <scratch>/before with python3 tools/build.py --out <scratch>/before --link, before any edit; the gate diffs the final build against it.

1. authoring-08. Fix the two front-matter traps instead of warning about them.
   Observation: tools/lib/frontmatter.loads treats 'title: Rose Knob  # a note' as the title 'Rose Knob  # a note' (comments are skipped only when a line starts with #, frontmatter.py:83), and a beta: block indented with tabs loads as {'beta': [{'label': 'Trailhead'}], 'value': 'Twin Lakes'} because indentation is measured with lstrip(' ') (frontmatter.py:85), so a tab counts as column 0 and the second key escapes to the top level. The validator catches neither (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:463-464, known limit 10.6); ADDING-TRIPS.md:163 only warns.
   Proposal: in frontmatter.loads (1) on a 'key: value' line strip a comment that starts with ' #' outside quotes and outside [brackets] (YAML's rule: # preceded by whitespace), leaving # inside quoted strings and in values with no space before it; (2) raise ValueError('line N is indented with a tab; use spaces') on any leading tab, which content.load_trip already surfaces as an ERROR naming the file. Tests in tests/test_frontmatter.py for both, plus # inside quotes and in inline lists, and a round trip with dumps (which already quotes strings containing #, frontmatter.py:136). Add the two lines to the error table in docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md 5.2 and delete the warning sentence at ADDING-TRIPS.md:163. Then run python3 tools/validate.py over today's content: it must still report 0 errors, 1 warning (no trip has a literal ' #' in an unquoted value today; if one does, stop and show it).
   Files: tools/lib/frontmatter.py, tests/test_frontmatter.py, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md.

2. authoring-15. First tests for the import tools (write the file now; later items add cases to it).
   Observation: nothing tests tools/add_trip.py (grep 'add_trip' in tests/ finds test_build_site's own helper of that name, three comments, and tests/test_ticklist_cli.py:664-676 asserting the tick-list CLI never calls it); tools/lib/strava.py is imported by no test; fit_reader.py only through read_gpx in tests/test_gpxroute.py:125; tests/test_render_goals.py:1-9 stubs render_map, fetch_osm, render_single and optimize_images. The path from a recording to a trip folder (FIT decode, simplify, stats, front matter, photo copies) runs only in a real import.
   Proposal: tests/test_add_trip.py: a synthetic 20-point GPX and a hand-built FIT (header, one definition message, three records, written with struct) in a temp dir; run add_trip.main() with TRIPS patched and --no-render; assert the folder and the keys of checks.INDEX_KEYS written, a well-formed track.gpx, --strip-times, --draft, --day-of numbering and its 'note:' lines, refusal of an existing folder without --force, and the Strava path against a synthetic activities.csv (two Distance columns, a Media list). Photo steps: stub photos.web_copies with a recorder that writes placeholder files the way tests/test_thumbs.py stubs thumb_copy and have_cwebp (test_thumbs.py:10-11; it stubs, it does not skip), or skip with skipTest when shutil.which('sips') is None (the CI runner has neither sips nor cwebp). tests/test_strava_export.py for Export.by_id, stats_from and paragraphs (strava.py:59, :70, :85). tests/test_fit_reader.py decodes the hand-built FIT through read_fit and read_track. No render smoke test (mapkit's drawing gets its own tests in Phase 15). Fixtures synthetic only (tests/test_fixture_text.py forbids real write-ups). Document the three files in docs/DEVELOPING.md 'What the test files cover'.
   Files: tests/test_add_trip.py, tests/test_strava_export.py, tests/test_fit_reader.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

3. gap8-01. Refuse --force on a trip that has a write-up or descriptions.
   Observation: tools/add_trip.py refuses an existing folder only without --force (:182-183). With --force it rewrites track.gpx and index.md from scratch (front matter from the track and the flags at :186-194, body '' or the Strava description at :155 with the placeholder comment at :195-196) and numbers photos from 01 (:193; add_photos :63-80, start=1), so photos/01.webp onward are overwritten. The existing index.md is never read, so a re-import of a published trip silently drops the write-up, every alt line, beta rows, map: peaks, cover_photo and focal point, card_summary, featured, search_aliases and published_at. The help says only 'overwrite an existing trip folder' (:126); ADDING-TRIPS.md's only --force is render.py's (:44); the validator cannot catch the loss because a bare trip is valid (checks.py:811-848; 76 of 168 trips have no write-up today). The folder is committed, so git checkout -- content/trips/<slug> recovers an erased write-up; the guard is against the silent case.
   Proposal: before writing, when <folder>/index.md exists, split it with frontmatter.split and refuse (exit 1, nothing written) when the body is non-empty and not the placeholder comment, when any photo entry has a non-empty alt, or when any of beta, map, cover_photo, cover_focal_x, cover_focal_y, card_summary, featured, search_aliases, published_at, updated_at, region_days is present; also refuse when days/ exists. Message: 'content/trips/<slug>/index.md has a write-up and N photo descriptions; --force would erase them. Move the folder out of content/trips/ first (git keeps the old one: git checkout -- content/trips/<slug> restores it).' (Phase 9 adds 'or use --update <slug>' to that message.) Keep --force only for a folder with no index.md or whose index.md is still the untouched import (placeholder body, empty alts), and print the files it will overwrite. Tests in tests/test_add_trip.py: a written-up trip from helpers.make_trip in a temp folder, add_trip.TRIPS patched, main() run with --force and a GPX string from tests/gpx_fixtures.py; assert exit 1, the message, and index.md unchanged byte for byte; a second case where the untouched import is overwritten. Document the rule in ADDING-TRIPS.md under 'If something goes wrong'.
   Files: tools/add_trip.py, tests/test_add_trip.py, ADDING-TRIPS.md.

4. authoring-13. Validate region and sub-type at entry and print the remaining steps.
   Observation: add_trip.py takes --region and --subtype as free text (:115, :117); a second spelling becomes a second region that only the validator's warning (checks.py:949-963, _region_spellings) catches afterwards; the validator checks subtype against content.OTHER_SUBTYPES (content.py:25, used at checks.py:640-643), not core.SUB_WORD, and add_trip.py imports tools/lib only (:28). The tool writes alt: '' for every photo (:78) and ends with one 'next:' line (:203); :166 counts existing day photos with a *.jpg glob although every photo file is .webp (1,004 .webp, 0 .jpg under content/trips/*/photos), so '(N existing)' is always 0. add_trip.py has its own slugify (:39-42, truncated to 60 chars) distinct from content.slugify (content.py:28-32), which checks.py:955 uses.
   Proposal: --subtype takes argparse choices from content.OTHER_SUBTYPES; the Strava path applies the same check to the sub-type strava.TYPE_MAP gives (Canoeing, Rowing, Snowshoe, Swimming are outside the list today): refuse with the same message or add them to OTHER_SUBTYPES and core.SUB_WORD/SUB_ICON (say which in the log). Load the live regions with content.load_all(include_drafts=True); when content.slugify(--region) equals the slug of an existing region spelt differently, stop with ap.error (exit 2) saying 'region "eastern sierra" is written "Eastern Sierra" on N trips; use that spelling'; a wholly new name is accepted as today, with one printed line 'new region: X (existing: ...)'. Replace the *.jpg glob at :166 with one that counts .webp and the .jpg fallback web_copies writes without cwebp. At the end print a numbered checklist instead of the one 'next:' line: the file to edit, how many alt: lines are empty, the optional beta/peaks lines (peaks need a re-render), then the preview command (item 8) and the publish steps as ADDING-TRIPS.md names them (Phase 8 replaces that line with the publish command). Update ADDING-TRIPS.md:39-46 to say the tool refuses a second spelling. Tests in tests/test_add_trip.py.
   Files: tools/add_trip.py, tests/test_add_trip.py, ADDING-TRIPS.md.

5. gap8-08. --day-of: count day photos right and bump end_date.
   Observation: --day-of always appends the next number (add_trip.py:163-164); :166 counts existing day photos with the *.jpg glob (always 0); when the new day's date is later than index.md's end_date the build only warns (checks.py:764-771) and ADDING-TRIPS.md:111-114 tells Eric to edit the line by hand.
   Proposal: (1) count existing day photos by the dNN- prefix across .webp and .jpg (glob photos/d*-*.webp and .jpg). (2) After --day-of, when index.md has an end_date earlier than the new day's date, rewrite end_date through the frontmatter round trip (byte-identical on all 352 index.md and days/*.md files today, measured by the review) and print 'end_date 2026-09-12 -> 2026-09-13' (the day file's own date from the track, no guess); when index.md has no end_date line, write none. Update ADDING-TRIPS.md:111-114 to say the line is updated for you. Tests in tests/test_add_trip.py with a helpers.make_trip multi-day fixture: the count, the bump, and no bump when the day is not later. Replacing a day's recording is Phase 9's --update; a PCT day still needs its day: label by hand (docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md Phase 3 'Remaining issues').
   Files: tools/add_trip.py, tests/test_add_trip.py, ADDING-TRIPS.md.

6. gap8-07. Fix a trip's time zone and recompute its date and start time.
   Observation: timezone, date and start_time are written once at import from tracks.local_start (add_trip.py:147, :188-189). guess_tz (tools/lib/tracks.py:87-95) knows Europe/Zurich, America/Los_Angeles and America/Denver and returns 'UTC' for everything else: one published trip carries timezone: UTC today, so its 'Start HH:MM' line (core.py:987) is a UTC clock, and Arizona, which keeps no summer time, falls in the Denver box. docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 12.12 lists more suspected zones in Eric's private file. The fix needs the UTC timestamps, which the published GPX keeps (156 of the 157 track.gpx files and all 184 days/NN.gpx hold <time>; tracks.py:72-79), so it runs offline; none of timezone, date or start_time is in the render hash (render.py:432), so no render follows.
   Proposal: (1) a --retime SLUG [--timezone Z] mode in tools/add_trip.py: read the trip's own track.gpx with fit_reader.read_gpx, call tracks.local_start(pts, zone or the file's timezone), set timezone, date and start_time through the frontmatter round trip, print old -> new, refuse a GPX without timestamps and a folder with days/. Typical use: add_trip.py --retime SLUG --timezone America/Chicago. (2) Extend guess_tz with America/Phoenix for Arizona's box (lat 31.3-37, lon -114.8 to -109), America/Chicago (lon >= -102 and < -85, lat > 25) and America/New_York east of -85, and have add_trip.py print the zone it used ('timezone America/Denver, guessed; --timezone to change'). (3) A validator note when timezone is UTC on a trip with a track: 'start_time is a UTC clock; set timezone: and run add_trip.py --retime'. Tests: a guess_tz table and the retime on a fixture GPX with a known timestamp in a new tests/test_tracks.py (tests/test_track_rules.py is about the generator). Which trips to re-time is Eric's list; do not guess. A changed date moves the trip in every newest-first list and the feed, which is correct.
   Files: tools/add_trip.py, tools/lib/tracks.py, tools/lib/checks.py, tests/test_tracks.py, ADDING-TRIPS.md (option table).

7. authoring-06 and authoring-07. Read Garmin's zip directly; list the Strava activities not yet on the site.
   Observation (authoring-06): ADDING-TRIPS.md:9 tells the author to unzip Garmin Connect's 'Export Original' download first; tracks.read (tools/lib/tracks.py:16-18) and fit_reader.read_track (fit_reader.py:151-152) dispatch on '.gpx' else FIT (gzip handled inside read_fit, :35-38), so a .zip fails. The review looked at a Garmin bulk path and dropped it (the account export's FIT members are named by upload id, not activity id, and join only by time); do not build one.
   Proposal: tracks.read: when the path ends in .zip, open it with zipfile, require exactly one .fit/.fit.gz/.gpx member under a size bound (say 50 MB), read it from memory (no extraction) and pass the bytes to fit_reader (add read_fit_bytes and read_gpx_bytes; read_fit and read_gpx call them). Test in tests/test_tracks.py with a temp zip holding a hand-built FIT or a small GPX, plus a zip with two members that must be refused with a message naming them. Change ADDING-TRIPS.md:9 and README.md to say the .zip from 'Export Original' can be given to add_trip.py as it is. No garmin_id key.
   Observation (authoring-07): 156 index.md files and 184 days/NN.md files carry a strava_id from the first bulk import (340 distinct ids) and nothing reads it (checks.py:60, :63). The current Strava export's activities.csv holds 721 activities (csv.reader; wc -l gives 772 because descriptions span lines), 603 .fit.gz and 118 .gpx tracks, 561 media files; 381 activities are on no trip or day. Finding one to import means reading the CSV by hand for --strava-id (ADDING-TRIPS.md:132-138); the guide gives only a placeholder export path (:137), which is right (the export is personal data outside the repository; keep the placeholder).
   Proposal: python3 tools/add_trip.py --strava-export DIR --list [--type Hike,Ride] [--since YYYY-MM-DD]: strava.Export.not_imported(known_ids) (beside by_id and search, strava.py:59-67) returns the records whose id is on no index.md or days/NN.md (collect strava_id with frontmatter.loads over content/trips/*/index.md and content/trips/*/days/*.md); print id, date, type, name, distance and whether media exists, newest first, and exit without creating anything. Test in tests/test_strava_export.py with a synthetic two-row activities.csv in a temp dir. Document the flag in ADDING-TRIPS.md step 2 next to --strava-id.
   Files: tools/lib/tracks.py, tools/lib/fit_reader.py, tools/add_trip.py, tools/lib/strava.py, tests/test_tracks.py, tests/test_strava_export.py, ADDING-TRIPS.md, README.md.

8. authoring-10 and perf-12. Preview by symlink, open the new page, no traceback on Ctrl+C; rebuild on change.
   Observation: build.py --serve copies about 240 MB of photos, GPX files and renders into _site/ on every preview (LINK is set only by --link, build.py:398; copy at :301-305); with iCloud offloading half of rendered/ that is the stall the docs describe (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:216-221; docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md:21-23: 29 min 54 s once), while a --link build measured 2.84 s. The author then types the trip's URL by hand, and ADDING-TRIPS.md:174-175 says the traceback after Ctrl+C 'is normal'; main() has no KeyboardInterrupt handling (build.py:409-414). Every edit to a write-up, a stylesheet or a page module means Ctrl+C, re-run, reload. build() calls shutil.rmtree(OUT) and recreates it before writing (build.py:272-274).
   Proposal: build.py: --serve implies --link (add --copy to opt out), accepts --open [SLUG] which runs macOS open on http://localhost:<port>/ or /trips/<slug>/ once the server is up, and catches KeyboardInterrupt around serve_forever() to exit quietly. Add --watch for use with --serve: a daemon thread polls every second (stdlib os.stat) the mtimes of files under content/ (for each photos/ folder its own mtime rather than its files), assets/, tools/sitegen/, tools/lib/ and rendered/site/, skipping conflict_copy() names (build.py:72), and re-runs build() when something changed, printing one line per rebuild; when the content validator raises, print the ERROR lines as main() does at build.py:402-405 and keep serving. State in the code and in ADDING-TRIPS.md's preview step (lines 170-175) that build() empties the output folder first, so a reload during the two-second rebuild can show a 404 and a second reload fixes it. add_trip.py's checklist prints python3 tools/build.py --serve --open <slug>. Update README.md:35 and ADDING-TRIPS.md step 4, and the --watch flag in docs/DEVELOPING.md. Tests in tests/test_build_cli.py: --serve with --copy still copies; the symlinked build is never used by CI (pages.yml runs plain --check).
   Files: tools/build.py, tools/add_trip.py, README.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md, docs/DEVELOPING.md, tests/test_build_cli.py.

9. gap9-03. Render into a temp folder and swap, so a failed render keeps the last good map.
   Observation: render_trip removes rendered/trips/<slug>/ before it draws anything (render.py:460) and writes meta.json last (:479); render_site does the same to rendered/site/ (:647). A terrain-tile failure raises RuntimeError in geo.get_tile (geo.py:205) and nothing in render_trip or main catches it, so the run stops with that trip's folder already gone; with --jobs the first exception re-raises from f.result() (:751), the other workers finish, the failing trip's folder stays half-written and later failures are never read. A Ctrl-C leaves hillshade PNGs that build.py copies by glob into the site (build.py:186) beside a page with no map (core.render_meta finds no meta.json, core.py:469-478); the validator then only says 'no rendered map' (checks.py:832-835). render_single, render_multi and set_out write through t['rendered'] (render.py:402, 406, 461, 471). os.replace of a folder over a non-empty folder fails with ENOTEMPTY (POSIX).
   Proposal: in render_trip and render_site bind the output to <folder>.tmp-<pid> for the whole drawing (set t['rendered'] to the temp path inside a try/finally and restore it after; add rendered/**/*.tmp-* to .gitignore), draw, run optimize_images, write meta.json, then swap: rename the old folder to <folder>.old, the temp folder to <folder>, and rmtree .old. In the finally clause remove any temp folder left, so a KeyboardInterrupt (not an Exception subclass) also cleans up. On an Exception print '! <slug>: <error>', keep the old render and go on to the next trip in both the serial loop and the pool loop; exit 1 at the end with the failed slugs. Cover it in tests/test_render_goals.py with the existing stubs: render_single raising leaves the previous folder's files intact and the run completes with exit 1; a BaseException leaves no .tmp folder. Add to ADDING-TRIPS.md 'If something goes wrong' (line 313): a render that fails leaves the old maps on the site.
   Files: tools/render.py, tests/test_render_goals.py, .gitignore, ADDING-TRIPS.md.

10. gap7-09. Guard the OSM cache against a corrupt answer.
   Observation: geo.overpass() returns a cached answer whenever the file exists, read with a bare json.load (geo.py:464-465); a truncated or unreadable cache file raises, render.fetch_osm (render.py:158-166) retries the same read three times with 10/20/30 s sleeps, then draws the map terrain-only and marks osm_failed, and the next run hits the same file again. get_tile already refetches a truncated tile (geo.py:206-211).
   Proposal: in geo.overpass() read the cached file inside try/except (ValueError, OSError): on failure remove it and fall through to the fetch, as get_tile does; one test with a temp OSM_DIR holding a truncated file and subprocess.run stubbed to return a good answer (tests/test_geo_cache.py). No new flag: add one line beside --force in README 'Check, build and re-render' and in ADDING-TRIPS 'If something goes wrong': to pick up newer OpenStreetMap data (a new trail, a renamed lake), delete .cache/osm/ (or the one answer) and run python3 tools/render.py --site --force; say that this refetches every answer (the log's 200 MB case, docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md:1014-1016) and can change roads and labels on every map, which the hash check does not notice. No automatic expiry.
   Files: tools/lib/geo.py, tests/test_geo_cache.py, README.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md.

11. gap9-06. Move the tile and OSM caches out of iCloud Drive and give answers a date.
   Observation: TILE_DIR and OSM_DIR default to <repo>/.cache/{tiles,osm} (geo.py:14-15), inside ~/Documents, which iCloud Drive offloads: on 2026-10-13, 206 of the 220 OSM answers and 1,806 of the 2,524 tiles were dataless stubs (1.3 GB logical), so a re-render blocks on downloads and fails offline (offline reads fall through to fetch(), geo.py:206-211). The caches never expire and the key is the sha1 of the query text (geo.py:461-465, :486-507). geo.py creates both directories at import (:16-17).
   Proposal: default TILE_DIR and OSM_DIR to ~/Library/Caches/personal-website/{tiles,osm} (outside iCloud Drive; excluded from Time Machine), keeping the TILE_DIR and OSM_DIR env overrides; create the folders lazily in get_tile and overpass rather than at import, so tests and CI do not create a folder under ~/Library. Store each new OSM answer as {'fetched': 'YYYY-MM-DD', 'query': ..., 'data': ...} and read the old bare form too; at the end of a run print the tile and OSM hit/miss counts and the oldest and newest OSM date used (Phase 15 also puts them into meta['renderer']). No --refresh-osm flag: a deliberate refresh is deleting .cache/osm (item 10). Document in docs/DEVELOPING.md:15 and ADDING-TRIPS.md's 'needs the internet' lines the one-time move Eric runs himself: mv .cache/tiles .cache/osm into the new folder (a plain mv makes iCloud download each file first, about 1.3 GB; do it before, not during, a re-render; it then occupies about 1.4 GB of local disk). The iCloud note in the workspace ../CLAUDE.md is outside the repository: tell Eric in the report rather than editing it.
   Files: tools/lib/geo.py, tools/render.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md.

12. gap8-05. The validator warns when a trip's maps are older than its inputs.
   Observation: checks.py:831-835 warns only when rendered/trips/<slug>/meta.json is missing. Nothing compares the hash stored there (render.py:455-458) with the current inputs: checks.py never mentions a hash, and the only hashing in sitegen/sitecheck.py (:31, :626) checks that a published GPX copy equals its source. So after a hand or github.com change of map: peaks, stats, activity, kind or the track, the build passes and publishes maps drawn from the old inputs, and ADDING-TRIPS.md:318-319 relies on Eric remembering to run render.py. A render that lost its OpenStreetMap data sets hash to null (render.py:475-477) and is equally invisible.
   Proposal: move VERSION, file_hash and trip_inputs out of tools/render.py into a new tools/lib/renderkey.py (no mapkit import; render.py imports it) and, in checks._trip_findings, when meta.json exists and its hash differs from renderkey.trip_inputs(t), warn 'maps were drawn from other inputs (the track, activity, kind, map: or stats changed since): run python3 tools/render.py <slug>'; when the hash is null or osm_failed is true, warn 'maps were drawn without OpenStreetMap data; run python3 tools/render.py <slug> again'; the same for rendered/site/meta.json as one warning (the site hash needs journal_parts, render.py:543; move it too or leave the site line for Phase 15, and say which). Measure the cost: file_hash reads every GPX on each validate run (341 files, 31 MB); cache per run if validate.py slows noticeably. Add both lines to docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md section 5 and tests in tests/test_checks_site_maps.py with a fixture meta.json carrying a wrong hash and a null hash. python3 tools/validate.py on today's content must print neither warning.
   Files: tools/lib/renderkey.py, tools/render.py, tools/lib/checks.py, tests/test_checks_site_maps.py, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md.

13. authoring-09. render.py --status: what a render would do, offline.
   Observation: render.py with no arguments draws everything whose input hash changed (render.py:1-11, :457) and nothing lists that set without the internet; main() builds the pending list inline (:733-735); drafts are never rendered and naming a draft slug says nothing (:711); add_trip.py starts the render with check=False and prints 'next: edit' whether or not maps were drawn (:201-203).
   Proposal: extract pending_trips(trips, slugs, force) from render.main() and site_maps_stale(trips) from render_site's hash (:638-644), and add python3 tools/render.py --status: one line per trip to be rendered and why (new; track changed; title/map/stats changed; OSM missing last time; no track; draft, skipped), then 'site maps: up to date | stale', exit 1 when anything is pending; it reads content/ and rendered/ only and fetches nothing. add_trip.py prints that status after its render and, with --no-render, 'maps not drawn: python3 tools/render.py <slug>'. Phase 8's publish.py will refuse a slug that --status names. Unit test with the drawing stubbed as tests/test_render_goals.py does.
   Files: tools/render.py, tools/add_trip.py, tests/test_render_goals.py.

14. resilience-12. An honest empty box for a report whose map is not rendered.
   Observation: a report with a track but no render (after --no-render, without internet, or a draft just un-drafted) still gets a #map section because has_map = bool(t['track']) (report.py:596): core.map_block returns an empty <div class="map map--report"> when meta['maps'] has no entry (core.py:484-527), yet the section keeps its key rows, the caption and the phone stats note (report.py:613-621), the title action 'View route' (:598-602) and the full-screen map page (:714). The goal page already handles the state with an .empty box 'Map not rendered yet' (report.py:394-409). The validator's 'no rendered map' line (checks.py:833-835) is a warning that does not stop the build, so a trip pushed after add_trip.py --no-render ships a blank map section. No live trip is in this state.
   Proposal: in report.page (report.py:596-623) and multiday.overview_map (multiday.py:381-393) test meta['maps'] as goal_map_section does: with no render, emit the .empty box ('Map not rendered yet; the GPX file is published') in the #map section, drop the key rows, caption, map preloads and the full-screen page for that trip (report.py:708-715, multiday.py:802-806), and keep the GPX download and the title action. Make the validator warning at checks.py:834 name the consequence ('the page shows an empty map box'). Add the fixture state to tests/test_report_page.py and to the file that holds the multi-day page tests (there is no tests/test_multiday_page.py; MultiDayPageTests is in tests/test_report_page.py:1967), following the 'report without a track' test at tests/test_a11y_static.py:1818.
   Files: tools/sitegen/pages/report.py, tools/sitegen/pages/multiday.py, tools/lib/checks.py, tests/test_report_page.py.

15. Docs sweep for the phase: ADDING-TRIPS.md step 4 (preview) and the option table (--timezone, --force, --retime, --list, the .zip input), README.md's preview line and 'Check, build and re-render' block, docs/DEVELOPING.md's file table for the new modules and tests.

What not to touch: nothing under content/ or rendered/ (resilience-12 changes no live page, since every published trip with a track has a render); no page module other than report.py, multiday.py for item 14; no CSS or JavaScript; no Garmin bulk path; no --refresh-osm flag; no change to the hash inputs beyond moving them into renderkey.py (a byte-identical hash function, so nothing re-renders).

Acceptance gate (run all of it and put the numbers in the report):
- /usr/bin/python3 -W error -m unittest discover -s tests and python3.12 -W error -m unittest discover -s tests pass with the new tests/test_add_trip.py (synthetic GPX and hand-built FIT; --no-render; --force refused and index.md byte-identical; --day-of count and end_date bump; --retime on a fixture GPX; a .zip with one member read, two members refused; --strava-export --list on a synthetic CSV), tests/test_frontmatter.py (' #' value; tab-indented block raises ValueError naming the line), tests/test_tracks.py, tests/test_strava_export.py, tests/test_fit_reader.py, tests/test_geo_cache.py.
- frontmatter.loads('title: Rose Knob  # a note') returns 'Rose Knob'; python3 tools/validate.py over today's content reports 0 errors, 1 warning, and prints neither stale-map warning.
- validate.py on a fixture whose rendered meta.json hash differs from trip_inputs, and on one with hash null, prints the two new warnings.
- With the network off, python3 tools/render.py --status lists nothing pending today, fetches nothing and exits 0; on a fixture with a changed track it names the trip and the reason and exits 1.
- python3 tools/build.py --serve --open <slug> builds with --link in under 5 s, opens the page, and Ctrl+C ends without a traceback; --serve --copy still copies; --watch rebuilds within about 2 s after an edit to an index.md and keeps serving after a validator error (test it by hand and say so).
- A fixture trip with a track and no meta.json builds an .empty 'Map not rendered yet' box in #map, no key rows, no caption and no trips/<slug>/map/ page, and build.py --check passes.
- In tests/test_render_goals.py a render_single that raises leaves the previous rendered folder's files intact, the run completes with exit 1, and no .tmp-* folder remains; a truncated .cache/osm file is removed and refetched (stubbed) instead of marking the map osm_failed.
- add_trip.py refuses a second spelling of an existing region (exit 2 with the existing spelling named), takes --subtype only from content.OTHER_SUBTYPES, prints a numbered checklist with the count of empty alt lines, and the '(N existing)' photo count is right for .webp files.
- python3 tools/build.py --out <scratch> --link --check passes; a diff -rq of two --link builds of today's content before and after the phase shows no page changed (resilience-12 reaches no live trip).

Screenshots: none needed; include the terminal output of --status and of the --force refusal in the report.

Log entry: 'Phase 6 - Author tools' in docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md with the format above. Under 'Remaining issues and author inputs' list: the one-time cache move for Eric to run; the workspace ../CLAUDE.md sentences to shorten (it is outside the repository); his private re-time list (12.12); the Strava --list output's 381 count as the backfill pool.
```

**Gate:** both Pythons pass with `-W error` including the new test files; `validate.py` still says 0 errors, 1 warning on today's content and prints both stale-map warnings on fixtures; `render.py --status` runs offline and exits 0 today; `--serve --open` builds linked in under 5 s and Ctrl+C is quiet; `--force` on a written-up fixture exits 1 and leaves `index.md` byte-identical; a failed `render_single` leaves the old folder intact and no `.tmp-*`; two `--link` builds of today's content before and after differ in no page; the log entry names every check with its Python.

## Phase 7 - Search and the explorer: vocabulary, matching, filters, cards and forms

The biggest visitor win that needs no decision: a typed search that finds 'lovers leap', 'skiing', 'june 2024' and 'loop', a search box on every page and the 404, a way out of zero results, and the explorer's facets, sorts, cards and phone forms. Everything lives in `tools/sitegen/search.py`, `assets/js/pages/explore.js`, `trips.js`, `ticklist.js`, `tools/sitegen/pages/trips.py`, `ticklist.py`, `notfound.py` and `core.related`. Independent of Phase 6.

### Prompt

```
PHASE 7: Search and the explorer: vocabulary, matching, filters, cards and forms.

Goal: make a typed search find what a route or peak searcher means (apostrophes, inflections, dates, route shapes, word starts), give zero results a way out, put a search box on every page and the 404, and sharpen the explorer's facets, sorts, cards and phone forms.

Defaults that stand unless Eric says otherwise in this session (ask once, at the start, in one message, and go on with the defaults if he does not answer): (a) gap3-01's activity word lists as written below, 'sup' included; (b) visual-02 (hide the duplicate browse block with scripting on) rather than ia-05 (reorder it and trim the regions); (c) visual-05's FILTERS_FROM = 6, counting active goals only.

Order: index and matching first (1-6), then the pages that use them (7-12), then cards and phone forms (13-20). Rebuild and re-measure search.json after item 5.

First build HEAD into <scratch>/before with python3 tools/build.py --out <scratch>/before --link, before any edit; the gate diffs the final build against it.

1. resilience-03. Drop apostrophes in both normalizers.
   Observation: normalize() (tools/sitegen/search.py:33-39) and norm() (assets/js/pages/explore.js:9-13) turn every non-alphanumeric run into a space, so "Lover's Leap" is indexed as 'lover s leap' (tests/norm_cases.json holds exactly that case). 'lovers leap', 'jakes peak', 'maggies', 'toms thumb' or 'didnt' get no result, although nine titles carry an apostrophe (three Lover's Leap reports, two Jake's Peak, Maggie's Peak, Tom's Thumb, "It's good to be back", "didn't see anyone else out"). The log's answer was an alias per title (docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md:215-216; docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md:397-398).
   Proposal: in both normalizers delete ', ’ and ‘ before the non-alphanumeric replacement (Python: re.sub("['’‘]", '', s); JS: s.replace(/['’‘]/g, '')), so title, index and query all become 'lovers leap' / 'didnt'. Update tests/norm_cases.json (the Lover's Leap cases and a didn’t case) and add a harness case in tests/trips_js_harness.js that 'lovers', "lover's" and 'lover’s' each find the Lover's Leap entries. trips.js has no norm() of its own (trips.js:15 takes it from Explore). Rewrite docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md:397-398, which advises an alias, and close the log item.
   Files: tools/sitegen/search.py, assets/js/pages/explore.js, tests/norm_cases.json, tests/trips_js_harness.js, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md.

2. gap3-01. Write the activity's inflections and synonyms into the keyword field.
   Observation: search.py:53-62 puts only the activity label (and the Other sub-type word) into the index's 'k' field before aliases and peaks; explore.js match() (44-47) needs every typed word as a substring of title+place+keywords+write-up, so an inflection or synonym only ever hits write-ups: over the 167 entries of _site/trips/search.json, 'skiing' 9 hits against 51 ski reports, 'skied' 6, 'touring' 2, 'bike' 10 against 61 rides, 'mtb' 2, 'hike' 13 against 33 hikes, 'climbed' 1, 'paddle' 7, and 'sup' 12 of which 9 are write-ups containing 'super'. The placeholder promises 'activity' (trips.py:128) and the status row (trips.js:65) then reads '9 OF 167 REPORTS' as if that were the answer.
   Proposal: one table in search.py, ACTIVITY_WORDS keyed by activity key plus SUBTYPE_WORDS keyed by the Other sub-type, holding the longest forms so the existing prefix match covers the short ones: ski -> 'skiing skied skis skimo skinning touring'; mtb -> 'mountain biking bikes rides riding mtb cycling'; hike -> 'hiking hikes hiked'; climb -> 'climbing climbs climbed'; SUP -> 'paddleboarding paddleboards paddling sup standup'; Rafting -> 'rafting rafts' (Kayaking, Mountaineering alike). entry() appends them to 'k' after the label, before aliases and peaks. Nothing in JavaScript changes. Keep the list to inflections and the sport's common names (no 'splitboard' on every ski report, no 'rock' on every climb) and show it to Eric in the report. Update tests/test_search.py:199-214 (they pin 'k' to the bare label), docs/DEVELOPING.md:280-283 (the 'k' contract) and CONTENT_GUIDE.md section 6. Expected cost: search.json 67,718 -> about 74,000 B raw, 17,196 -> about 17,400 B gzip.
   Files: tools/sitegen/search.py, tests/test_search.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md.

3. gap3-02. Match typed words at word starts, not inside words.
   Observation: explore.js match() (46), score() (51-53) and snippet() (64), trips.js dayHits (41) and the write-up-only test (334), and ticklist.js matches() (37) all use indexOf, so a token hits anywhere inside a word. Over search.json: 'ice' 16 hits of which 15 are inside 'nice', 'practice', 'price', 'office', 'noticed', 'sliced', 'choice' (word-start: 1); 'ice climbing' 4 hits, none containing the word ice; 'tal' 26 (word-start 20: Tallac kept, 'total', 'mental', 'brutal' dropped); 'rain' 8 -> 6 ('terrain', 'training'). The harnesses pin 'matter' -> Matterhorn (trips_js_harness.js:160), 'for' -> 'ford' (:259) and 'coul' (ticklist_js_harness.js:415) as 'substring'; all three are prefixes.
   Proposal: add has(field, tok) to explore.js: (' ' + field).indexOf(' ' + tok) !== -1, and use it in match(), score(), snippet() (its per-word loop becomes w.indexOf(tok) === 0), trips.js dayHits (title and text at line 41) and the write-up-only check (334), and ticklist.js matches() over data-text. Index files unchanged. Harness cases: 'ice' must not hit 'practice', 'tal' still hits 'tallac', 'for' still hits 'ford'; ticklist 'coul' unchanged. Reword DEVELOPING.md:324 ('every token as a substring') and CONTENT_GUIDE.md:396 and :432 ('has to appear somewhere' -> 'has to start a word'). Known loss: 'horn' no longer finds Matterhorn (2 hits today, 0 at word start); say so in the docs.
   Files: assets/js/pages/explore.js, assets/js/pages/trips.js, assets/js/pages/ticklist.js, tests/trips_js_harness.js, tests/ticklist_js_harness.js, docs/DEVELOPING.md, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md.

4. gap3-03. Index each trip's year and month names.
   Observation: search.entry() (search.py:47-65) indexes no date word although the date is content (content.py:557 reads date and end_date) and is printed on every card: '2024' 1 hit (the PCT title), '2025' 0, 'june' 1 (the place 'June Lake Loop'), 'july' 0, 'september' 0. The Year select exists, but the box is where a visitor types 'june 2025 tallac'.
   Proposal: in search.entry() append to 'k' the words derived from the trip's own date: the year (both years when date and end_date differ) and the full English month name of every month in date..end_date (a tuple of full names in search.py; 'jun' then prefixes 'june'). No day-of-month for trips (a bare digit prefixes every year) and no ski-season tokens (the season's second half is a bare two-digit number). DEVELOPING.md:75-80 forbids invented publication dates, not the trip's displayed date; say so in the rule. Cost about +1.7 KB raw, +35 B gzip. Tests: the 'k' pins in test_search.py:199-214; CONTENT_GUIDE.md section 6 list.
   Files: tools/sitegen/search.py, tests/test_search.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md.

5. gap3-07. Index the route shape words.
   Observation: content.py:559 reads route_shape and core.trip_tags (core.py:373-374) shows it on cards and title rows; the front matter holds 71 'loop', 73 'out-and-back' and 15 'point-to-point' (14 published). search.entry() leaves it out, so 'loop' finds 11 (8 titles with Loop, the place 'June Lake Loop', 2 write-ups) of 71 loops.
   Proposal: append normalize(route_shape) to 'k' ('loop', 'out and back', 'point to point'); 'loop' then finds all 71 at keyword score and 'out and back' ranks the 73 first under Best match. No change to tokens() or explore.js (tokens() is shared with the tick list; no stop words). Tests: test_search.py; CONTENT_GUIDE.md section 6; DEVELOPING.md:280-283.
   Files: tools/sitegen/search.py, tests/test_search.py, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

6. gap3-04. Let a journal day be found by its date.
   Observation: search.day_entry() (search.py:73-94) carries the day's date only as display text 'w' ('Apr 22, 2024'); trips.js dayHits (26-52) matches tokens on the day title 't', the write-up 'x' and 'day N' only, with the journal's words riding along (:42), so 'june 2024', 'may 22' or 'pct june' list no PCT day although journal.json holds 150 days (Apr 10, May 31, Jun 30, Jul 31, Aug 30, Sep 18). In dayHits a token found both in a day's own field and in 'q' counts as neither own nor riding (trips.js:43-44), and the PCT's 'q' already holds '2024' ('pacific crest trail 2024 pct'), so a year inside a day field would let '2024' alone list all 150 days.
   Proposal: add a 'd' field to day_entry: normalize of the full month name and day of month only ('may 22'), never the year, which stays a journal-level word in 'q'. In dayHits treat a word-start hit in 'd' as the day's own (own = true, +1, below a title hit's 3). Then 'june 2024' lists the 30 June days (five, then the existing 'Show all 30 journal days' button, trips.js:70-73), 'may 22' finds Day 31, 'pct june' works, 'june' alone lists the June days and '2024' alone lists none. Harness: give the DAYS fixture (tests/trips_js_harness.js:229-235) a 'd' and add cases including '2024' alone -> []; test_search.py day-entry contract; DEVELOPING.md:328-340 and CONTENT_GUIDE.md:425-446 ('the date is not searched' changes). journal.json (94,751 B today) grows about 2-3 KB raw. Do it after item 3 so 'd' is matched at word starts.
   Files: tools/sitegen/search.py, assets/js/pages/trips.js, tests/trips_js_harness.js, tests/test_search.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md.

7. gap3-05. Make the placeholder and the description say what is searched.
   Observation: trips.py:127-128 writes the label 'Search trips' and the placeholder 'Search by name, place, region, activity or peak'; the page description (trips.py:188) adds 'write-up'; the write-ups are the largest field (23.2 KB of search.json's 67.7 KB) and 92 of 167 reports have one, but nothing on the page says words from them can be typed ('spring' 3 hits, 'skied' 6, 'super' 9 are write-up-only today).

Keep the sr label 'Search trips' and the noscript note (trips.py:136-137). Align CONTENT_GUIDE.md section 6's list; tests/test_trips_page.py (which pins the sr label at 358-359) gains a pin for the new text.
   Files: tools/sitegen/pages/trips.py, tests/test_trips_page.py, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md.

8. ia-02. Make the header's Search a real search box that lands on the explorer.
   Observation: the header's Search is a link to trips/#search (core.py:1650; trips.js:499-507 focuses the box after the page loads). Apart from trips/ and the tick list's own goal search (#tkl-q), no page has a search field: not the 404, the Map page or the region pages. trips.js reads q from the query string on load (KEYS, trips.js:12-16) and the explorer form submits to itself (action="", trips.py:126). A URL that carries only q does not switch to Best match: trips.js:125 sets 'relevance' only for text typed into the page's box, and the harness asserts that adopt('?q=tallac') keeps sort 'newest' (tests/trips_js_harness.js:777-779).
   Proposal: in core.header replace the desktop Search link with <form class="nav-search" role="search" action="<core.link(here, 'trips/')>" method="get"><label class="sr" for="nav-q">Search trips</label><input id="nav-q" name="q" type="search" placeholder="Search" maxlength="200" autocomplete="off"><input type="hidden" name="sort" value="relevance"><button type="submit" aria-label="Search">icon</button></form>; the hidden sort is what makes a landing search rank by Best match, and clearing the box afterwards falls back to Newest through clearedSort (trips.js:142). Replace the menu's existing Search row (core.py:1663) with the same form (ids must differ: nav-q / menu-q). Keep the .nav-search class so base.css:178 still hides it under (scripting: none). On trips/ itself keep today's link (the page has its own box). Style: 36 px input of about 160 px that widens on focus, 44 px submit target; the header fits at 1140 px. No base.js change. Never echo q into HTML. Update tests/test_nav.py:72, 107, 111, 132 (link assertions become form assertions, action relative via core.link), tests/test_a11y_static.py:2317-2330, 2408, 2441 (a.nav-search becomes form.nav-search; label present), and docs/DEVELOPING.md:399-400.
   Files: tools/sitegen/core.py, assets/css/base.css, tests/test_nav.py, tests/test_a11y_static.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

9. seo-14. A search box and a slug-based suggestion on the 404 page.
   Observation: the 404 page (served with status 404 for any missing path, checked live) offers the home button, the five activity rows, Trips and Map (notfound.py:23-45); a visitor arriving from an old or mistyped trip link has no way to search from it. trips/?q= is a real URL the explorer reads.
   Proposal: add to the 404 body a <form role="search" action="trips/" method="get"> with a 48 px name="q" box and a button (the existing <base> resolves it) and the hidden sort=relevance of item 8. notfound.js, when location.pathname contains /trips/<slug>/, prefills the box with the slug's words (hyphens to spaces) and shows 'Looking for "<words>"?' above it; the words come from the URL only, never from a guess at a trip. 10. qa-07. Zero results offer the nearest working query.

**Gate:** the stdlib script over the built indexes gives the counts above ('lovers leap' 3, 'skiing' 51, 'bike' 61, 'ice' 1, 'loop' 71, 'june 2024' 30 days, '2024' no day) and `search.json` stays under 80 KB raw; both harnesses and both Pythons pass with the new cases; a report page's header form lands on `trips/?q=…&sort=relevance` with results; Trips says 14 REGIONS; the Tallac page's More like this lists Powderhouse once; at 390 px the first Trips card is on the first screen and the tick list shows no form over one goal; the before/after captures exist and the log names them.

## Phase 8 - Publishing loop, CI and recovery

Replaces the 14-action publish with one command that runs a linked check, stages by path, refuses conflict copies and unrendered trips, commits and (with `--push`) watches the deploy; writes the rollback, lost-index, web-edit and new-Mac recipes that have never existed; cleans the checkout and settles where it lives; makes a content push live in about a minute, pins Node, parses the bundle in CI, and lets a `draft: true` push take a trip off the site from anywhere. Needs Phase 6 (`render.py --status`, the stale-map warnings).

### Prompt

```
PHASE 8: Publishing loop, CI and recovery.

Goal: one publish command that stops on failure; the recovery recipes; a cleaner checkout; a faster content push; a build that can take a drafted trip off the site without a render.

Decisions to ask Eric at the start (one message; go on with the recommended answer if he does not reply):
(a) Where the checkout lives. Recommended: move the clone out of iCloud Drive (for example ~/Code/personal-website; GitHub holds everything tracked; .cache/ and _site/ are rebuilt) and run git gc only after the move. The alternative is to stay in ~/Documents, mark the folder Keep Downloaded in Finder, and run tidy.py before each commit. The move itself is his to do by hand; this phase changes the documents that name the path.
(b) docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md decision 12.8: should the build drop a drafted or deleted trip's own pin and line from the region maps itself (item 22), so a draft: true push from a phone takes a trip off the site? Recommended yes; clusters stay as rendered until the next render.

Order: the publish command and its watch first (1-2), then the developer command (3), the recovery docs (4-8), the Mac checks and cleanup (9-11), the docs and the suite timing (12-16), CI (17-21), and the build-side drop last (22).

First build HEAD into <scratch>/before with python3 tools/build.py --out <scratch>/before --link, before any edit; the gate diffs the final build against it.

1. authoring-01. One publish command that runs the publish steps and stops on failure.
   Observation: publishing a single-day trip is 14 manual actions (export and unzip, gather photos, cd, add_trip, edit index.md, fill alt lines, build --serve, Ctrl+C, build --check, git add three paths, git status --short, read the outputs, commit, push, wait). ADDING-TRIPS.md:197 says the three publish commands 'run one after the other whatever each one answers, so a failed check does not stop the git add'; the branch check (:249-254) and the conflict-copy reading (:242-247) are by hand; README.md:37-60 repeats the commands; there is no tools/publish.py, no Makefile and no git hook (.git/hooks holds only *.sample files). A plain build.main(['--check']) copies about 240 MB into _site/ (the iCloud stall); --out DIR --link --check takes about 4 s and check() reads OUT (build.py:325-328). The per-trip flow has not been run once yet (trip index.md files were added in two bulk commits), so the first real uses should end at the commit with the staged list on screen.
   Proposal: tools/publish.py SLUG [SLUG…] [--message TEXT] [--dry-run] [--push] [--allow-unrendered]. It refuses unless git branch --show-current is main and nothing unrelated is already staged; runs build.main(['--out', '.cache/publish-site', '--link', '--check']) (build.py:376; --check already runs checks.run through content_errors(), build.py:399) and stops on the first failure; refuses when python3 tools/render.py --status (Phase 6) names a slug being published unless --allow-unrendered; stages exactly content/trips/<slug>, rendered/trips/<slug> when the folder exists, rendered/site when git status --porcelain rendered/site is non-empty, and content/home.json when changed; refuses any staged path for which sitecheck.conflict_copy(basename) (sitecheck.py:55) is true; prints the staged list; commits as 'Add <title>' from the front matter (or 'Update <title>' when the folder already had commits) and then prints the git push line. With --push it also pushes and hands over to item 2; --dry-run does everything but commit. Never git add -A. Tests in tests/test_publish_cli.py against a temporary git repository with a synthetic trip from tests/helpers.py (git is on the runner), in the style of tests/test_build_cli.py. Replace ADDING-TRIPS.md step 5 and README.md step 3 with the one command and keep the manual commands under 'What it does' so a wrapper failure never blocks publishing.
   Files: tools/publish.py, tests/test_publish_cli.py, ADDING-TRIPS.md, README.md.

2. authoring-02. Watch the deploy from the terminal after the push.
   Observation: ADDING-TRIPS.md:249-250 says the trip is 'live a few minutes later' and names no way to see whether the run passed; a failed run leaves the old site up with only the Actions page or its email as the signal. gh 2.97 is installed at /opt/homebrew/bin/gh and signed in; gh run list takes --commit and --json, gh run watch has --exit-status; the last five runs took 151-188 s.
   Proposal: after publish.py --push: find the run for HEAD with gh run list --commit <sha> --json databaseId,status --limit 1 (retry for up to 30 s while it appears), then gh run watch <id> --exit-status; on success print the live URL of each published trip (core.SITE_URL + 'trips/<slug>/'); on failure print gh run view <id> --log-failed and the sentence that the old site is still up. Without gh, or when gh is not signed in, print the Actions URL and exit 0: the watch never fails a publish. No --status subcommand: document gh run list -L 3 in ADDING-TRIPS.md step 5 as the way to see the last runs.
   Files: tools/publish.py, ADDING-TRIPS.md.

3. authoring-17. One developer command for both Pythons and the three checks.
   Observation: docs/DEVELOPING.md:1288-1295 asks for the suite on /usr/bin/python3 and python3.12 with -W error before any change to tools/; /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.12 (3.12.11) exists; nothing runs the three steps together. The first deploy failed on a runner patch release the Mac does not have (docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md:945-951), which no local hook catches, so no hook.
   Proposal: tools/check.py (stdlib): runs python3 tools/validate.py, then /usr/bin/python3 -W error -m unittest discover -s tests, then the same on python3.12 when it is on PATH (otherwise one line saying brew install python@3.12 and continue), then python3 tools/build.py --check --out .cache/check --link; one summary line per step with its wall time and a non-zero exit when any step fails; --quick skips the second Python. Document it in docs/DEVELOPING.md 'Checking your work' as the command to run before a push that touches tools/, tests/ or assets/.
   Files: tools/check.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

4. gap7-01. The written, rehearsed rollback.
   Observation: no guide says how to go back after a bad push (a grep of README.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md and docs/*.md for revert/rollback/undo finds only docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:864-866, 'Going back, not rehearsed'). Facts that fix the recipe: a push to main is live about 2.5 to 3 minutes later (the last five runs 151-188 s); the github-pages environment admits runs whose ref is main only (custom branch policy, one entry); a workflow dispatch takes a branch or tag name, never a commit sha, and there is no tag; deploy-pages ships only its own run's artifact. Two paths remain: gh run rerun <id of the last good run> (whole run, not --failed) rebuilds that commit without a commit, is offered by GitHub for 30 days after the run, and is undone by the next push; git revert --no-edit <sha> && git push is the durable one. Both take about 3 minutes and both sit in the pages concurrency group with cancel-in-progress (pages.yml:16-18), so a rollback push cancels a bad run still in flight. Force-push is ruled out by docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md section 6. Any run before d7272e0 fails the tests on the runner's Python 3.12.15, so a re-run of an older run is not a rollback.
   Proposal: add 'The site is wrong after a push' to ADDING-TRIPS.md 'When something goes wrong' (item 6) with a cross-reference from docs/DEVELOPING.md: (1) the durable fix is git revert --no-edit <sha> then git push (for several commits, revert each newest-first in one go); the deploy takes about three minutes; a push cancels a run still in progress, so do not wait for a bad deploy to finish; (2) the temporary fix with no commit is gh run rerun <id> with the id of the last good run from gh run list (whole run), offered for 30 days and undone by the next push; (3) never force-push and never rewrite history; (4) watch with gh run watch or the Actions page. Rehearse it once in this session on a harmless doc-only commit (Eric must say yes to that push in this session; otherwise write the recipe and mark the rehearsal as pending) and write the measured minutes into the doc. No --undo tool.
   Files: ADDING-TRIPS.md, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

5. gap7-02. Let a workflow dispatch build any past commit on main.
   Observation: workflow_dispatch is on (pages.yml:9) but takes no input, and the checkout step (pages.yml:24, actions/checkout@v4 with no ref) always takes the pushed commit, so a manual run can only rebuild main's head; the one commit-free rollback is re-running an old run within 30 days.
   Proposal: add inputs: ref: {description: 'commit to build and deploy (empty: this branch)', required: false} under workflow_dispatch, and with: ref: ${{ inputs.ref || github.sha }} on actions/checkout@v4. The run itself stays on main, which is what the environment policy checks, while the build job runs that commit's tests and build.py --check before deploying it. Command for the doc: gh workflow run pages.yml -f ref=<sha>; say it is temporary (the next push re-deploys main's head), must be followed by a revert, and that only someone with write access can dispatch it. Try it once with the current head's sha (that deploys the same site again; still ask Eric first since it is a deploy) to confirm the environment gate accepts it; if it is refused at the deploy job, say so in the doc and keep only the re-run path.
   Files: .github/workflows/pages.yml, ADDING-TRIPS.md.

6. gap7-10. Turn 'If something goes wrong' into a recovery section.
   Observation: ADDING-TRIPS.md:313-332 has six symptom bullets (cwebp, OSM busy, a changed title, a missing .th copy, Strava arguments, a missing render folder) plus a pointer to the other docs; all six are hiccups before the push. Four of the ten Actions runs on record failed: three on 2026-09-27/28 at actions/configure-pages ('Get Pages site failed', a one-time repository setting that has not recurred) and one on 2026-10-12 at the unit tests (docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md:945-951); the deploy job was skipped each time, so the old site stayed up. No guide says how to read the red X, that the old site stays up, or that a local build can take minutes while iCloud downloads offloaded files (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:219-221); the index incident is at :476-484.
   Proposal: rename the section 'When something goes wrong' and group it in three parts, one bold symptom per entry and the fix in two or three lines. Before the push: the six existing bullets, with the OSM bullet moved to The Mac and the docs pointer kept as the last line. After the push: 'The Actions run shows a red X' (the old site stays up; open the run and see which step of the build job stopped: Unit tests, where the log's first FAIL: or ERROR: line names the test, fix locally, run the suite, push again; Build, where the first ERROR or BROKEN line or 'link check: N problems' names the file, fix locally, run build.py --check, push again; actions/configure-pages, where 'Get Pages site failed' means the repository's Pages setting on GitHub, Settings > Pages > Source: GitHub Actions, is off, nothing to fix locally); 'The site is wrong after a push' (item 4); 'I need an old version back for a moment' (re-run or item 5); 'A trip I drafted is still on the site' (items 8 and 22). The Mac: 'The build takes minutes instead of seconds' (iCloud has offloaded rendered/: Finder's Keep Downloaded on the folder, or python3 tools/doctor.py from item 9); 'git says the index is missing or unreadable' (item 7); 'A map has no roads or names after a render' (the OSM bullet plus Phase 6's delete-the-cache line). Keep every entry to the symptom the author sees.
   Files: ADDING-TRIPS.md.

7. gap7-07. Document and detect the lost .git/index recovery.
   Observation: on 2026-10-10 iCloud removed .git/index while the disk was at 13 GB; the recovery (git read-tree HEAD after comparing the tree; history, branches, objects and working tree intact) is written only in docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:476-484. .git/ holds eight stray copies, index 2 to index 9, two made on 2026-10-13, so it recurs.
   Proposal: the entry 'git says the index is missing or unreadable' in item 6: run git read-tree HEAD, then git status --short; nothing is lost but the staging list, so git add again what was staged; then free disk space before going on. doctor.py (item 9) flags a missing .git/index with that recipe and lists the stray index N copies with the rm line to run by hand after confirming each is not the live index. Deleting the copies is Eric's call.
   Files: ADDING-TRIPS.md, tools/doctor.py.

8. gap7-04. Warn that drafting a trip on github.com can fail the deploy.
   Observation: ADDING-TRIPS.md:256-262 'Quick fixes from anywhere' says an edit to index.md on github.com redeploys the site on its own. Adding draft: true there to one of the 27 trips with a pin of its own fails build.py --check in CI, the trip stays up, and the only signal is the Actions page; the removal section (:264-284) describes the failure but never says the web path is affected.
   Proposal: two sentences under 'Quick fixes from anywhere' and 'Taking a trip off the site': a web edit that adds draft: true or links to another report can fail the check and change nothing on the site; look at the Actions page (or gh run list --limit 1) after a web edit. If item 22 lands in this session, invert it: the web edit becomes the recommended way to take a trip off from a phone.
   Files: ADDING-TRIPS.md.

9. gap7-05. tools/doctor.py: one read-only check of the Mac.
   Observation: what the Mac needs is spread over README.md:9-10 (cwebp), ADDING-TRIPS.md:315, docs/DEVELOPING.md:1288-1302 (two Pythons; 53 tests skip without node), the workspace CLAUDE.md (conflict copies, offloaded files) and the incident at docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:476-484; nothing checks any of it. Measured 2026-10-13: cwebp 1.5.0, node 25.8.2, /usr/bin/python3 3.9.6, python3.12 3.12.11, gh 2.97 signed in, git identity set, about 18-20 GB free of 228, 6,136 conflict copies under rendered/ and assets/ (0 tracked; 6,102 of them offloaded), 0 tracked files offloaded, eight .git/index N copies. The macOS dataless flag (st_flags & 0x40000000) is readable with os.lstat.
   Proposal: python3 tools/doctor.py, stdlib, 3.9-compatible, read-only, offline, one line per check (OK/WARN and the fix): disk free in GB, WARN under 30 GB citing the October incident; .git/index present, else the git read-tree HEAD recipe; stray .git/index N copies listed with the rm line to run by hand; branch is main, tree clean, ahead/behind origin/main from the tracking ref, identity set; conflict copies counted by top folder, with any under content/ or among staged files named; tracked files under rendered/ and content/ that are offloaded (macOS os.lstat().st_flags & 0x40000000; 'not checked' elsewhere), with Finder 'Keep Downloaded' as the fix; cwebp present (brew install webp), node and python3.12 optional with one clause each. Exit 1 only on what blocks a publish (not on main, dirty tree, index missing). No --network, no cache or validate probes. Tests: a synthetic repo in a temp folder for the git and copy checks, the disk and offload probes stubbed. Mention it in README 'Check, build and re-render', in the recovery section and in the new-Mac section (item 11).
   Files: tools/doctor.py, tests/test_doctor.py, README.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

10. authoring-16 and gap10-05. tidy.py for conflict copies; the cleanup commands; where the checkout lives.
   Observation: on disk under rendered/ sit 6,134-6,136 conflict copies beside 3,393 tracked files (git-ignored by .gitignore:7-19 for rendered/ and assets/; the validator names those inside a trip folder, checks.py:559-576, as errors), plus .git/index 2 to index 9 and two empty _site/* 2 folders. The checkout occupies about 1.4 GB (logical 1.5 GB) of synced space: .git 222 MB (pack 182.75 MiB plus 36.42 MiB in 4,749 loose objects; about 13 MB saved by a gc), the tracked tree 232 MB, _site/ 240 MB (wiped and rewritten by every copying build, so iCloud re-uploads it each time), .cache/ 717 MB of which only osm (284 MB) and tiles (77 MB) are render cache and about 356 MB are review evidence folders (review, p4, p5, plan2, preview). .cache/p4/PRIVACY_AUTHOR_ONLY.md is where docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md:224-225 tells Eric to keep an author-only file; .cache/plan2 holds this review's evidence. build.py:272-273 empties the output folder on every build; a folder under ~/Library/Caches passes unsafe_out (build.py:357-373). The cause of the copies, the offloading and the index incident is the checkout syncing through iCloud Drive with storage optimization.
   Proposal: (1) tools/tidy.py (stdlib): list every file and folder that sitecheck.conflict_copy accepts under rendered/, assets/, content/ and _site/, and the .git/index N files, one line each; for a copy under content/ compare bytes with its original and mark 'identical', 'older' or 'DIFFERS'. --delete removes only the copies under rendered/, assets/ and _site/ (git-ignored, never built); copies under content/ and the .git/index N files are reported with the instruction to delete them by hand (ADDING-TRIPS.md:246). tests/test_tidy.py on a synthetic tree. (2) One-time cleanup commands for Eric, written in the report and in README.md: delete .git/index 2 to index 9; git gc once free disk is well above 20 GB, nothing else is writing, and ideally after the move; after this plan is accepted, move .cache/review, p5, preview and the non-privacy parts of p4 to the scratchpad or delete them, keep .cache/p4/PRIVACY_AUTHOR_ONLY.md where PRIVACY_NOTES.md:225 says it lives, leave .cache/plan2 until the plan is accepted; state in docs/DEVELOPING.md that .cache/ holds the render caches plus the one author-only privacy file. (3) Document under README.md:114 and docs/DEVELOPING.md:1264 the commands that keep the 240 MB build out of iCloud using what build.py already has: python3 tools/build.py --out ~/Library/Caches/personal-website/site --link --check and the same with --serve; _site/ stays the CI path; no new default, no env var. (4) The location decision: if Eric moves the checkout, update ADDING-TRIPS.md:19 and docs/TICK_LIST_GUIDE.md:139 (both hard-code the iCloud path) to 'the folder you cloned the site into', shrink the conflict-copy paragraphs in README.md:54-57 and ADDING-TRIPS.md:232-247 to one sentence, and tell him the workspace ../CLAUDE.md needs the same (outside the repository); if he stays, the paragraphs stay and tidy.py is named before each commit.
   Files: tools/tidy.py, tests/test_tidy.py, README.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md, docs/TICK_LIST_GUIDE.md, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

11. gap7-06. The new-Mac setup and what a fresh clone refetches.
   Observation: README.md:9-10 says nothing needs installing but cwebp; ADDING-TRIPS.md:19 and docs/TICK_LIST_GUIDE.md:139 hard-code the iCloud path; docs/DEVELOPING.md:1288-1302 wants python3.12 and node; no page lists the Xcode command-line tools (git and the 3.9.6 python3), Homebrew, gh auth login, the size of a clone (a 182.75 MiB pack plus about 172 MB of content/; about 205 MB over the wire after GitHub repacks), or that .cache/ (284 MB of Overpass answers, 77 MB of tiles) is git-ignored and refetched on the first render. On a new Mac render.py without --force redraws nothing (the hashes exclude OSM data) and --site --force redraws all 24 site maps from today's OSM.
   Proposal: a 'Setting up a new Mac' section in README.md (eight lines): xcode-select --install; install Homebrew; brew install webp gh node python@3.12 (the last two optional, say why); gh auth login; git clone https://github.com/esichak/personal-website.git into the folder item 10 settled on, never a Finder copy of the old folder (it would carry the conflict copies); python3 tools/doctor.py; python3 -m unittest discover -s tests; python3 tools/build.py --check. Then one paragraph: the first render.py downloads about 360 MB into the cache folder and nothing re-renders until a track, title or map setting changes; --site --force redraws every region map from current OSM data. Close with what is not in the clone (item 12). Known: docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 15.4 point 1 lists the hard-coded paths.
   Files: README.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md, docs/TICK_LIST_GUIDE.md, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

12. authoring-20. Say what is and is not in the repository.
   Observation: content/ (1,698 tracked files, 1,004 photos) and rendered/ (3,393) are on GitHub, so every published trip, track and web photo copy is backed up; git status --ignored --short shows nothing untracked outside _site/, .cache/, the rendered/ conflict copies and six .DS_Store files. Not in the repository: the original photos handed to --photos (add_trip.py:63-78 writes only the 1600 px, 800 px and card-size WebP copies, tools/lib/photos.py:149-169, EXIF stripped), the original .fit/.gpx (the published track.gpx is the simplified track, add_trip.py:148 and :185), and the author-only file under .cache/, which is the one thing in the checkout that is neither on GitHub nor re-fetchable. No document says any of this.
   Proposal: one short 'What is and is not in the repository' paragraph in README.md beside the Privacy section and one sentence in ADDING-TRIPS.md step 5 saying add_trip.py never copies the originals, so the source photos and the track file should stay wherever Eric keeps them, and naming the author-only file under .cache/ as the one thing to back up himself. No originals/ folder in the checkout (GPS-bearing originals in a public checkout under iCloud pressure), no new flag, no .gitignore or privacy_audit.py change.
   Files: README.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md.

16. authoring-11. The two-builds diff recipe.

Builds are deterministic apart from the footer's © year (core.py:1696-1698); .cache/ is accepted by --out (build.py:354).

17. authoring-03. Run the unit tests in CI only when code changed; skip docs-only pushes.
   Observation: pages.yml runs the whole suite before every build (:28-31); in run 38429837834 the Unit tests step took 113 s of a 137 s build job, the build 4 s. The tests build synthetic trips in temp folders and never read content/ (tests/helpers.py:1-6), so a push that adds a trip waits almost two minutes for tests it cannot affect; a docs-only commit also triggers a full test, build and deploy of an unchanged site. One test reads repository files outside tools/ and tests/: tests/test_privacy_audit.py checks docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md (:1337-1340) and the workflow files (:1331-1334). fetch-depth: 2 would leave github.event.before unfetched when a push holds more than one commit; a schedule run has no github.event.before.
   Proposal: in .github/workflows/pages.yml keep actions/checkout@v4 at its default depth; add a step 'changed' that sets code=true when the event is workflow_dispatch, when github.event.before is all zeros (first push, force push), when git fetch --no-tags --depth=1 origin ${{ github.event.before }} fails, or when git diff --name-only ${{ github.event.before }} ${{ github.sha }} touches tools/, tests/, assets/, .github/ or docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md; 'Unit tests' gets if: steps.changed.outputs.code == 'true'. Add paths-ignore under on.push for Markdown that the build never reads (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md, docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md, docs/personal-website-improvement-plan.md, the root *.md), never docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md, content/ or rendered/. No scheduled run. Note in docs/DEVELOPING.md:1247-1251 which pushes run the tests. Expected: a content push live in about a minute instead of three. Check one content-only run with gh run list after Eric's next push.
   Files: .github/workflows/pages.yml, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

18. perf-13. Pin Node in CI.
