```
   Proposal: add actions/setup-node@v4 with node-version: '22' before the 'Unit tests' step and note the pinned version beside the Node sentence in docs/DEVELOPING.md (about line 1300). Nothing else: no build sha in the pages (it would make every local build differ from live) and no post-deploy curl.
   Files: .github/workflows/pages.yml, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

19. resilience-08. Parse the script bundle in CI.
   Observation: build.js_bundle() concatenates base.js and eight page scripts with a bare newline (build.py:93-99); one SyntaxError anywhere stops the whole file, taking the menu, units switch, lightbox and every page script with it. node --check runs only on the harness files (tests/test_maplist_js.py:108, test_ticklist_js.py:111); section.js is read only as text by tests/test_a11y_static.py:1978-2031; multiday.js and notfound.js appear in no test; nothing parses the built bundle (node --check _site/assets/site.js passes today, run by hand).
   Proposal: one unit test (pattern: tests/test_maplist_js.py:100-109, skipped when node is not on PATH) that writes build.js_bundle() to a temporary file and runs node --check on it, and runs node --check on each assets/js/pages/*.js. No sitecheck line, no Python fallback parser.
   Files: tests/test_build_cli.py (or a new tests/test_js_bundle.py).

20. a11y-11. ARIA id references and heading order in build.py --check.
   Observation: sitecheck.py verifies links, anchors, duplicate ids, metadata, sitemap, feed, indexes and GPX bytes (:242-698) and nothing about ARIA or headings; the static a11y tests run on a synthetic fixture (tests/test_a11y_static.py:64-65). 22. gap7-03. The build drops a gone trip's pin and line from the region maps (only if Eric said yes to decision b).
   Observation: rendered/site/meta.json shows 160 trips drawn on the 24 site maps; 27 have a pin of their own on at least one map (a cluster of one), 133 only ever merge into a numbered cluster. Drafting or deleting one of the 27 fails build.py --check with 'BROKEN map/index.html -> ../trips/<slug>/' until render.py has run with the internet, so the trip stays up; the other 133 pass but keep their line. Each pin is <a class="mk-pin" href="@@ROOT@@trips/<slug>/" data-key="<slug>"> and each line a <g class="mk-cat" data-key="<slug>"> with no nested <g> (mapkit.py:1531, 1538); every fragment passes through core.frag (core.py:450-462), which does plain string replacement; base.js:142, 174 only query by data-key, so a missing key is harmless; checks.py:987-1018 only warns; docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:512-514 is decision 12.8. The site meta is read four ways (build.py:271 uncached into register_site; the cached core.site_meta() for home and region_groups; archive.site_meta() re-reads for archive and region builds; map_block(site_maps=True) reads the file itself), and core.region_groups already intersects a map's keys with the listed trips.
   Proposal: pins and lines only, no cluster rewriting. In core.map_block(site_maps=True) compute gone = keys in that map's trips (and journals, when a journal part's trip is gone) that are not a published trip with a track (core.has_track) and not an active planned goal; pass it to core.frag(path, here, ns, drop=None), which removes each <a class="mk-pin" … data-key="K" …>…</a> and each <g class="mk-cat…" data-key="K" …>…</g> (neither nests its own tag; covers mk-planned), and warns once per map when a key in drop is not found so a markup change in mapkit is noticed. Leave clusters as rendered: a merged pin keeps the gone trip's title in its tooltip and count until the next render, and the check's WARNING says so. Reword checks._site_map_findings: the build leaves the trip's own pin and line out; run python3 tools/render.py --site when online to redraw the map's frame, merged pins and counts; update tests/test_checks_site_maps.py and the docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md row at line 212. Tests: a synthetic overlay from mapkit's exact strings through frag(drop=…) (pin and line removed, cluster untouched, unknown key warns), and a built-site check that a drafted own-pin trip passes --check. ADDING-TRIPS 'Taking a trip off the site' shrinks to: draft: true or delete the folder, build.py --check, commit, push, from anywhere; the render only tidies later. Two --link builds of today's content before and after must be byte-identical (nothing is gone today).
```

## Phase 9 - Content: inventory, editing a published trip, and places for Eric's words

Gives Eric a per-trip table of what each report lacks and the places, titles and taxonomy to fix; tools to change a published trip without hand surgery (a better recording, new photos, titles without a 76-file site render, publication dates); and the dormant slots for his own words (About body and portrait, region introductions, summits, year totals, home region maps) that show nothing until he writes. Needs Phase 6 (the add_trip tests, the frontmatter fix, `--retime`). The content work itself (79 `place:` lines, 24 retitles, merges) is an afternoon of Eric's, not the session's.

### Prompt

```
PHASE 9: Content: inventory, editing a published trip, and places for Eric's words.

Goal: a read-only content report; add_trip.py --update and trip_photos.py; titles written into site-map labels at build time; one 'Changing a trip that is on the site' table; honest publication dates; a source word that follows the content; dormant slots for Eric's words. Nothing on the live site changes unless Eric writes content, except the title label mechanism (byte-identical today) and the About By-year block.

Decisions to ask Eric at the start (one message; the recommended answers stand if he does not reply, except the merges and retitles, which are his edits and happen only if he says so):
(a) Region and place merges (item 4's listing shows them): recommended Idaho into Boise and Kingsbury Grade into Kingsbury (keep the spelling that has a region page; regions/boise/ exists, regions/idaho/ does not); Yosemite stays separate from Eastern Sierra. A merge needs render.py --site --force later; queue it for Phase 15 and say so.
(b) Retitle the 24 Strava default titles by hand from the report (item 2): recommended yes; after item 9 a title change costs one trip render, not 76 site files.
(c) published_at: recommended option A: add_trip writes an empty published_at: line that Eric fills on the day he pushes; feed pubDate and sitemap lastmod use it when set; existing reports stay dateless (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 12.5, 12.6).
(d) Summits field vocabulary (item 15): summit / turned-around / traverse.
(e) --update replaces stats by default, with --keep-stats to keep a hand correction; no --strip-times on update while docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md 6.2 is open.
(f) Which two regions the home page maps (item 18): recommended keep Lake Tahoe and Eastern Sierra (the Eastern Sierra map now carries the PCT days).
(g) The By-year block (item 16): calendar years, with the ski row linking the explorer rather than inventing a season total.

Order: the inventory first (1-5), then the editing tools (6-9), then the dates and the source word (10-12), then the dormant slots (13-18).

First build HEAD into <scratch>/before with python3 tools/build.py --out <scratch>/before --link, before any edit; the gate diffs the final build against it.

1. copy-07. A per-trip content report.
   Observation: validate.py -v lists 467 notes (79 'no place', 75 'no write-up', 61 'no photos', 109 'no title' for PCT days, 143 'N photos without a caption'; checks.py:723, 838-845), one line per finding with no per-trip view. Nothing reports that 103 of the 106 photo covers are automatic picks (3 cover_photo lines in content/), that 61 trips have no photo, that card_summary is set on 0 trips, that 89 trips have a place and 13 name a party, or which titles are Strava defaults. docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md:98-99 says 'An automatic pick is a fallback, not an editorial choice' but gives Eric no list.
   Proposal: tools/content_report.py (stdlib, read-only, reuses content.load_all and content.resolve_cover): one row per published trip, newest first: date · slug · title flag (Strava default / shared xN) · write-up chars · photos · cover (explicit / auto / none) · place · party · card_summary. Options --missing place|writeup|photos|cover|summary, --alt (item 3), --taxonomy (item 4) and --markdown (writes .cache/content-report.md, git-ignored). Demote checks.py:723's per-trip 'photos without a caption' note to one count line so -v stays readable. The tool never writes to content/. Document it in docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md next to :98-99.
   Files: tools/content_report.py, tools/lib/checks.py, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md, tests/test_content_report.py.
```

## Phase 10 - Report and multi-day pages: layout, facts, captions and the profile readout

Puts the photo and the words where a reader lands (photo-only reports get their hero, written reports without photos lead with the words, multi-day pages get a lead photo and a day strip), makes the facts strip and captions complete on phones (elapsed time, clock times, contour interval, key rows that decode OSM trails), unifies the section labels, and adds the hover and touch readout on the elevation profile. The modules are `tools/sitegen/pages/report.py` and `multiday.py`, `core.py`'s strip, caption and key helpers, `assets/css/pages/report.css` and `multiday.css`, and one new script. Needs Phase 9's ia-19 (the stats footnote link to about/#abo-how-h) for items 8-9 and the gate; otherwise build that link here. maps-01 part 3 (contour labels in both units inside the renders) waits for Phase 15.

### Prompt

```
PHASE 10: Report and multi-day pages: layout, facts, captions and the profile readout.

Goal: the photo and the words where a reader lands; a complete facts strip and honest captions on phones; one label per section; an elevation readout under a finger or a pointer.

Decisions to ask Eric at the start (one message; the recommended answers stand if he does not reply, except item 4, which waits for the pictures):
(a) report-02, report-01, report-11: the lead photo on photo-only reports (27 pages); the write-up above the map on written reports without photos whose write-up clears 100 characters; a lead photo and day strip on multi-day pages. Recommended yes to all three. These reverse plan 9.1 (docs/personal-website-improvement-plan.md:309) and the Phase 3 note (docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md:405-406; DEVELOPING.md:457-461) only where their reason, not manufacturing content, does not apply; say so in the log.
(b) report-03: the desktop hero frame, chosen from a side-by-side at 1280 px built in this session (item 4): the 3:2 cap (recommended), the photo's own shape clamped to 72vh, or today's 2:1 band.
(c) copy-04 and visual-03: a 'Track only' status tag on the 39 bare trips (reverses docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md:164's blank slot) and a factual head over 'More like this' instead of the selection rule (reverses a Phase 3 choice). Recommended yes to both.
(d) Times of day: elapsed time in the strip plus a clock column in the mile table and the GPS-max time, all read from the published GPX (recommended); a derived finish time (start + elapsed) waits on docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md 6.2.

Order: layouts (1-4), the strip, captions and keys (5-12), labels, tags and the head (13-15), the day nav, the rail, tables and the goal page (16-19), the readout last (20). Build the before captures first, and HEAD into <scratch>/before with --link.

   Observation: 35 single-day reports have photos and no write-up. narrative() needs both a write-up and a cover (report.py:23-26), so they keep the compact order and their photos come after the strip, map and chart (:639-642); their cover is still the card and share image (core.share_photo, core.py:1514-1522), so a reader who opens a shared link lands on a page whose photo is below the fold. 27 of the 35 have a cover that passes report.lead_photo (at least 6:5, :29-37); the other 8 have a portrait or square cover and stay as they are.
   Proposal: show the hero whenever lead_photo(t) exists, write-up or not: title, hero, strip, map, profile, remaining photos. In page(): show_hero = (story or has_gallery) and not lone and lead_photo(t) is not None; the cover leaves the grid as now (:591-594), photos_anchor=not has_gallery still covers a single landscape photo, and the map preload gives way to cover_preload (:657-660). Bottom bar unchanged (compact path, :342-351). Add a photo-only case to tests/test_report_page.py (test_photos_without_a_writeup_stay_compact at :410 asserts today's behavior) and a line to DEVELOPING.md:481-491 (the three cases become four). Re-measure one of the 27 pages with the DEVELOPING.md:1428 method (phone LCP, image bytes) before and after.

4. report-03. Stop cropping a third of every 4:3 lead photo on desktop.
   Observation: the lead photo frame is 3:2, 4:3 under 560 px and 2:1 from 1200 px with object-fit: cover (report.css:129, 135, 138). 43 of the 45 stories with a lead photo use a 4:3 photo, so at 1248 px a 1600x1200 photo is drawn 1248x624 and loses a third of its height; on the Matterhorn report the focal point (cover_focal_y: 40) keeps the spires and drops the sky and snowfield (desktop-report-matterhorn.jpeg). series.py:511 reuses report.hero, so the PCT lead crops the same way. With max-height: min(72vh, 840px) a 900 px window gives 648 px, close to today's 624, so the 'natural' variant gains only on tall windows.

5. resilience-06. Let phone galleries keep a panorama's or tall portrait's shape.
   Observation: under 760 px the gallery grid gives every landscape a 3:2 box and every solo portrait 4:5 with object-fit: cover (base.css:470-478), whatever the photo's shape; the --ar custom property that photo_rows writes on each link (core.py:930-931) is used only by the desktop rows. Content holds 7 landscapes wider than 2:1 (up to 3.14:1) and 5 portraits narrower than 0.6 (0.46), so a 2.8:1 panorama shows about half its width on a phone until the viewer opens. No written report's lead photo is wider than 2:1.
   Proposal: in the phone block of base.css: .jph--l img { aspect-ratio: var(--ar) } (the ratio is inline, so the box is sized before the file arrives and nothing shifts) and .jph--solo img { aspect-ratio: max(var(--ar), 0.6) } (if max() inside aspect-ratio proves unsupported in the cascade reader or a browser, emit a clamped --arp from photo_rows instead). Keep 3:4 for paired portraits. One cascade case in tests/test_a11y_static.py for the two selectors at 390 px. Leave .rep-hero and photo_partition alone.
   Files: assets/css/base.css, tests/test_a11y_static.py.

Phones hide every strip sub-line (report.css:48), so a phone reader never sees the start time (phone-bare-ride.jpeg). core.strip (core.py:1026-1033) writes every cell as <div class="strip-c"> with no per-cell class.

Extend core.strip's cell tuple with a modifier class (strip-c--time) so report.css shows that one sub-line on phones inside the phone block (:37-51): body.p-report .rep-strip .strip-c--time .strip-s { display: block }. stats_note gains 'Elapsed includes stops.' 7. report-05. Clock time in the elevation table and at the GPS max.
   Observation: elevation_table (report.py:172-198) reads the track with fit_reader.read_gpx, which returns a timestamp per point (fit_reader.py:129-149), and 156 of 157 single-day tracks carry <time>, but the table prints Mile · Elevation only; the profile marks the GPS max but not when it was reached (:222-223). All 156 declare timezone; tracks.py:11 already imports ZoneInfo with a fallback. docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 12.12 says a few trips' zones look wrong (Phase 6's --retime is the fix), so clock times on those trips are wrong until Eric re-times them; say so in the log.
   Proposal: in elevation_table keep the point index j and, when tr.raw[j][3] is set, add a third column 'Time' in the trip's zone (zoneinfo.ZoneInfo(t['timezone']), formatted by core.ftime); omit the column when no point has a time or the zone cannot be loaded (never fall back to UTC). In profile_section append 'AT h:mm AM' to the GPS MAX item from the timestamp of tr.imax under the same conditions. Single-day pages only; a multi-day day's GPS max lives in rail_stats (multiday.py:608-611) and waits for 12.12. Because the column is read from the published GPX, it disappears by itself if Eric later strips times. Tests: a fixture track with times and one without.
   Files: tools/sitegen/pages/report.py, tests/test_report_page.py.

Acceptance gate (run all of it and put the numbers in the report):
- tests/test_report_page.py rewritten: a photo-only fixture gets the hero (27 live pages change; list them in the log), a written fixture without photos and over core.STRUCTURED_MIN_CHARS takes the story order without a hero (count the live pages), one under the floor stays compact, a multi-day fixture has report.hero plus a thumb strip linking #day-N; DEVELOPING.md:457-497 describes four cases and records the reversal of plan 9.1 and the Phase 3 multi-day note.
- Measured with the DEVELOPING.md:1428 method on one photo-only report and the Haute Route: phone LCP under 2.5 s and the image bytes recorded before and after.
- report-03: the three variants built at 1280 px for the Matterhorn report and the PCT and shown side by side before the pick; the chosen frame pinned by a CSS or style test.
- Strip and table: the Moving cell's sub-line shows ELAPSED and START and is visible at 390 px (strip-c--time rule); the elevation table has a Time column on the 156 timed tracks and none on the untimed one; the profile meta carries 'GPS MAX … AT hh:mm'; the stats footnote ends with the link to about/#abo-how-h that sitecheck verifies, and on phones the note shows under the strip instead of under the map caption (rep-note-ph gone from the build).
- Captions and keys: 'Contours every 200 ft' | '61 m' per visible variant through core.U; the key row's Mile item reads 'Mile (1.6 km)' under KM; 'Trail (OSM)' and 'Road' key items appear only where the visible render's base layer draws them (fixture base files for both cases); 'VERTICAL SCALE ×N' in every chart head.
- One label table drives section_nav, bottom_bar and multiday's bar; 'Trip details' and 'Route' no longer appear in any built page (grep); the 39 bare trips show a 'Track only' status tag on cards, rows and the title block; More like this heads read e.g. 'EASTERN SIERRA · BACKCOUNTRY SKI'; tests/test_related.py:418-427 retargeted.
- maps-04: tests/profile_js_harness.js covers the readout; data-pf adds about 1.5 KB per report (measured); Chrome touch emulation shows the page still scrolls over the chart (touch-action: pan-y) and the readout hides on pointerleave and Escape; the map dot is left for later.
- A beta row with url: renders <a rel="noopener"> and a non-http scheme fails validate; every multi-day day chart and the section featured chart carry a 'View as table' details with core.U units; the goal page says ≈ distance once and its one-cell strip is capped (strip--1).
- Phone galleries: a 2.8:1 landscape and a 0.46 portrait fixture keep their --ar at 390 px in the cascade test; multi-day day chips show WRITE-UP / N PHOTOS and the Overview meta counts them.
- Both Pythons with -W error and build.py --out <scratch> --link --check pass; before/after captures at 390 and 1280 px of the Matterhorn report, back-to-clipless, hit-the-three-piece and the Haute Route are kept in .cache/plan2/phase10/.

Screenshots: the four pages above at both widths, before and after; the report-03 side-by-side sheet; the Matterhorn report at 390 px showing the footnote under the strip; one capture of the profile readout.

Log entry: 'Phase 10 - Report and multi-day pages'. Under author inputs: the reversals Eric confirmed, the frame he chose, the 12.12 time zones that make clock times wrong until re-timed, the derived finish time waiting on 6.2.
```

**Gate:** the four layout cases pass in `tests/test_report_page.py` and the 27 photo-only pages and the written-without-photos pages are listed in the log; the measured phone LCP on one photo-only report and the Haute Route stays under 2.5 s; the report-03 pick was made from a side-by-side sheet; ELAPSED and START show at 390 px, the mile table has a Time column, the footnote shows under the strip on phones and links the method; 'Contours every …' and 'Mile (1.6 km)' appear through `core.U`; 'Trip details' and 'Route' are gone from the build and the 39 bare trips carry 'Track only'; the profile readout harness passes and a touch emulation still scrolls; both Pythons pass with `-W error`.

## Phase 11 - The PCT journal, the photo viewer and the full-screen map

Shows the journal's 98 written-day photos as photos, tells a reader where an entry sits in the hike and lets them come back to it, fixes the dead July and August tabs, makes the photo viewer behave on phones (Back closes it, swipes stick, the first photo is announced, the share sheet opens), and lets the full-screen map open on the route with a scale and disclaimer in view, before any script runs. Needs Phase 10 (`core.photo_rows` is the row component report-18 reuses; the label table). No decision from Eric; the session decides report-18's sizes cap from the measurement.

### Prompt

```
PHASE 11: The PCT journal, the photo viewer and the full-screen map.

Goal: journal photos as photos; an entry's place in the hike and a way back to it; pressable month tabs; a photo viewer that behaves on phones; a full-screen map that opens on the route without a script. No decision from Eric is needed before starting.

Order: the journal (1-6), the viewer (7-9), sharing (10), the full-screen map (11-12), the real-phone look (13).

1. report-18. Show journal photos as photos, not squares, on written days.
   Observation: every journal day's photos are a contact sheet of 1:1 crops from the 320 px .th copies (series.py:321-338 thumbs(); series.css:181-189, .ser-ph img aspect-ratio 1 / 1 with object-fit: cover, 3 to 6 columns). The 32 written days hold 98 of the series' 103 day photos (the other 5 sit on three track-only days, 049, 050 and 059), and the default write-ups-only view (series.py:485 data-default="wu"; series.js:134) shows them first, cropped square at about 115-145 px, while single-day reports lay the same kind of photo out in justified rows (core.photo_rows, core.py:890). The sheet was chosen so a short last row does not trail off (series.css:179-180) and so the journal never loads the 800 px .sm file (series.py:322-323). .ser-body is about 930 px wide at 1200+ (base.css:123, series.css:233) and only .ser-wu is capped at 620 (series.css:178); photo_rows's col/jmax/jmin only set each image's sizes attribute while the real clamp lives in base.css:460-461 (--jmax 624, --jmin 280); core.photo serves 800w/1600w, so a 620 px landscape on a 2x screen fetches the 1600 px file. The viewer already groups by .jrows (base.js:296, :323-331); tests/test_series_page.py:900 and :911 assert the .ser-body children as ['ser-wu', 'ser-phs', 'ser-wnav'] and ['ser-phs'].
   Proposal: for written days (series.py:98 written(); the thumbs call at :413) use core.photo_rows(t, here, d['photos'], per_row=3, col=620, jmax=400, jmin=200, ns='d'+id) inside .ser-body, and in series.css add .ser-body .jrows { max-width: 620px; --jmax: 400px; --jmin: 200px; } so the block matches the write-up's measure and the declared sizes are the widths drawn; keep the square .th sheet for the three track-only days with photos; under 760 px base.css:469-475 already turns .jrow into the 2-column grid. Decide the bytes from a measurement: either accept that a 620 px landscape on a 2x desktop fetches the 1600 px file (lazy loading keeps the first paint unchanged) or give photo_rows a sizes cap so journal photos stop at the .sm copy, the trade-off docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 12.1 already accepts on phones; say which in the log. Update tests/test_series_page.py:900 and :911, the thumbs() docstring and the series.css comment. Measure the journal page's image bytes on the phone profile in the default write-ups-only view before and after (today the .th copies total 3.2 MB and the .sm copies 9.4 MB for all 103 photos).
   Files: tools/sitegen/pages/series.py, assets/css/pages/series.css, tools/sitegen/core.py (only if the sizes cap is added), tests/test_series_page.py.

2. a11y-07. Stop disabling the month tabs under the write-ups filter.
   Observation: with 'Show days with write-ups only' on (the default), series.js apply() (87-99) sets aria-disabled="true" and tabindex="-1" on the month links whose month has no write-up (July, Days 71-101, and August, Days 102-132) and series.css:119-120 removes their pointer events, so neither a mouse nor a keyboard can open those months from the sticky bar. reveal() (series.js:101-109) and the hashchange listener (:126-130) already turn the filter off and announce; a reveal is not stored (:110-112). No test pins the month links' aria-disabled.
   Proposal: in apply(), instead of aria-disabled/tabindex add a class .is-empty and an .sr suffix ', no write-ups' to the month link; keep the lighter color via .ser-mnav a.is-empty (replacing series.css:119-120). A press then follows the hash, reveal() unchecks the box, announces 'Showing all 150 days' through #ser-live, and scrolls to the month. Add a Node check that no .ser-mnav link carries aria-disabled or tabindex after apply(); update the filter description at docs/DEVELOPING.md:660. Known 15.4.6 (a hash into a hidden day overrides the stored choice) stays.
   Files: assets/js/pages/series.js, assets/css/pages/series.css, tests/test_series_js.py, tests/series_js_harness.js, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

3. features-07. Journal rows show recorded distance so far.
   Observation: each day row shows Distance, Gain and Moving for that day (series.py stats_dl, :341-350) and the strip gives the total 'Distance recorded' (:151), but no row says how far along the recording a day is. All 150 day files carry distance_km; gap rows ('DAYS N · NO RECORDING', gap_row :447-449) explain the breaks. The desktop row grid is nine fixed columns at two breakpoints (series.css:202, 228) and .ser-st a three-column grid there (:216, :230); on phones .ser-st wraps with dot separators (:160-164).
   Proposal: compute the cumulative recorded distance in day order and add a fourth cell 'So far' to stats_dl through core.U in both units, with the column header text 'recorded so far' in .ser-cols so it never reads as trail mileage. CSS: a tenth column in --ser-c at both breakpoints and a fourth column in .ser-st; no phone rule. No SVG line. Tests: the running sum, both units, and that gap rows do not change it.
   Files: tools/sitegen/pages/series.py, assets/css/pages/series.css, tests/test_series_page.py.

4. benchmark-10. Journal entries say their place in the hike.
   Observation: a written entry links only to the previous and next write-up (written_nav, series.py:380-396); the month heads give the month's days, write-ups and distance (:452-462) and the By-month index rows (:235-255) the same per month; nothing says which write-up of the 32 the reader is on or how far along the recording the month ends. The month nav already jumps to the first and last month, so no First/Last links.
   Proposal: written_nav adds one Mono-S item 'WRITE-UP 12 OF 32' (index in the written-days list it already builds); month_section meta and the month_index rows add 'N MI SO FAR' (cumulative recorded distance through the month's last day, core.U, the same prefix sum as item 3). series.css for the two items; tests check the counter and that the last month's figure equals the strip's total.
   Files: tools/sitegen/pages/series.py, assets/css/pages/series.css, tests/test_series_page.py.

5. features-08. A local 'Continue reading' bookmark.

The Node harness builds its own window object (tests/series_js_harness.js:447-456) with a sessionStorage stub (:450) and no localStorage; tests/page_standin.js serves only the trips and ticklist harnesses.

6. visual-20. One primary action on the PCT landing.
   Observation: the series title block offers three outline buttons of equal weight (Read the first written entry, Browse all recordings, Copy link; series.py:503-505), while every other page type marks one primary action in ink.
   Proposal: give the first action btn btn--ink at series.py:503; nothing else changes. Update any series test that pins the button classes.

7. resilience-10. Photo viewer: Back closes it, a failed photo says so.
   Observation: the viewer is a <dialog> opened with showModal() (base.js:288-363); it pushes no history entry, so on a phone the system Back gesture leaves the page instead of closing it (reasoned from the code; a real phone was never run, docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 15.5). lbImg.src = a.getAttribute('href') (base.js:311) has no error listener, so a photo that fails to load shows the broken-image glyph with no message; the bar holds previous, next and close only (core.py:1704-1709) and the viewer already shows the full-size file. tests/page_standin.js models history, pushState and popstate (:14-25, from :185); tests/maplist_js_harness.js is the harness that loads base.js.
   Proposal: on open, try { history.pushState({lb: true}, '') } catch (e) {} (as archive.js:48 does for file://); close the viewer on popstate when it is open; when closed by the button, Escape or a tap outside, call history.back() only if history.state && history.state.lb, with a flag so the popstate-driven close does not call back() again. Add an error listener on .lb-img that writes 'This photo could not be loaded' into .lb-cap and reveals a link to the href ('Open the file') only then; no fourth always-visible control. Cover open, close, Back and the error path in a harness case against tests/page_standin.js (switch the maplist harness to it for this case, or add the case to a harness that uses the stand-in). Confirm the Back gesture on a real phone once (item 13).
   Files: assets/js/base.js, tools/sitegen/core.py, assets/css/base.css, tests/maplist_js_harness.js, tests/page_standin.js.

8. gap1-07. Make the viewer's swipe reliable on touch screens.
   Observation: the viewer reads a swipe from pointerdown/pointerup with a 40 px threshold (base.js:345-356); the dialog declares no touch-action (none anywhere in base.css; the .lb rules are :719-730), nothing locks the document's scroll while the modal is open (body.menu-open at :230 serves the menu only), and pointercancel is not handled, so a one-finger drag the browser may treat as a page scroll ends in pointercancel and the swipe is dropped. The viewer was checked for keyboard operation and focus return only (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:295, :301); nothing ran on a device. setPointerCapture would retarget clicks to the dialog and break the bar's buttons and the tap-outside close (:358), and the full-viewport dialog already keeps the pointer.
   Proposal: in base.css: .lb { touch-action: pinch-zoom; } (one-finger drags reach the script; pinch-to-zoom stays) and html:has(dialog.lb[open]) { overflow: hidden; } (the stylesheet already relies on :has(), base.css:110). In base.js at :346-356: ignore non-primary pointers (if (!e.isPrimary) return; on both handlers) and add lb.addEventListener('pointercancel', function () { sx = null; }); nothing else. Verify in Chrome's touch emulation on a report with three or more photos (swipe with vertical drift, pinch, tap prev/next/close, tap outside).
   Files: assets/js/base.js, assets/css/base.css.

9. a11y-14. Announce the first photo when the viewer opens.
   Observation: base.js announces 'Photo N of M. <alt>' through #lb-live only when the viewer moves; on opening it calls show(i) without announce (the comment at :297 says so deliberately) and focuses the Close button (:325-328), so a screen-reader user hears 'Photo viewer, dialog, Close, button'. The bar already holds a counter, a caption and three 44 px buttons in 288 px at 320 px wide, so no fourth control; the file is reachable with a modified click (:321-323).
   Proposal: on open, call show(index, true) (or set lbLive.textContent in the open path) after showModal() so the first photo's 'Photo 1 of N. <alt>' is announced, and give the dialog aria-describedby="lb-live"; keep Escape, focus return and the bar as they are; update the comment at :297; one assertion in tests/test_a11y_static.py for the describedby. Phase 13's VoiceOver pass checks for double-speaking.
   Files: assets/js/base.js, tools/sitegen/core.py, tests/test_a11y_static.py.

10. phone-06. Share a report through the phone's share sheet.
   Observation: on phones the only sharing control is 'Copy link to this page' at the end of a report (report.py:70-73; base.js:365-426) and the Copy link button in the PCT actions, which writes the address to the clipboard or shows a field; phones have navigator.share and the site never offers it. navigator.share needs https and a user gesture (both true on GitHub Pages); the receiving app draws the page's og tags, so the 62 reports without an og:image show a text card (12.4); desktop browsers also expose navigator.share, so gate on (hover: none).
   Proposal: in the base.js click handler, when navigator.share exists and navigator.canShare({url}) passes, call navigator.share({title: document.title, url}) inside the click, in try/catch, and fall back to the clipboard path only when it rejects with anything but AbortError; at load, when navigator.share is present and (hover: none) matches, relabel the control 'Share this page' and swap the icon (add 'share' to tools/lib/icons.py). The markup keeps 'Copy link' for desktops and the control stays hidden without scripting. Apply to the per-entry copy links on the PCT page too. Harness case for the gate; tests in tests/test_report_page.py for the markup.
   Files: assets/js/base.js, tools/lib/icons.py, tools/sitegen/core.py, tests/test_report_page.py, tests/maplist_js_harness.js.

11. resilience-13. Open the full-screen map on the route without JavaScript.
   Observation: the full-screen map page (trips/<slug>/map/, linked from the phone map's expand button at core.py:523-524) is a scrollable region (div.fs-map, overflow:auto, base.css:374) holding the 1440 px wide render (.map width 1440px, :376); base.js:428-459 scrolls it to the route after the deferred bundle runs and nothing else sets the position, so without JavaScript, or before the script runs on a slow phone, the map opens at its left edge. Parsing the first .mk-trk path of the 157 rendered trip overlays, 141 have the route start right of x=390 and 98 have the whole route right of x=390, so a 390 px phone opens on no route for most trips. On ski maps mapkit.py:969-971 writes the descent .mk-trk first and the dashed ascent second; multi-day maps may carry a faded stroke-opacity: .35 ghost at index 0 (:979); the PCT series fs page has no .mk-trk at all (its route is .gl-trk/.mk-cat); the expand href is assembled at core.py:524 from fullscreen_href passed at report.py:400, :620, multiday.py:390 and series.py:297; sitecheck.py:275-276 fails any cross-page #fragment missing from the target's ids; route paths use only M and l with no <g transform> around them; .mv is position: relative with the SVG at inset: 0 (base.css:330-332).
   Proposal: in core.map_block (core.py:484-527), when 'fs' in sizes, find the route start in the inlined overlay (core.frag, :450-462): the first 'M x y' of the first .mk-trk path that is neither faded (stroke-opacity: .35) nor, on ski maps, the descent: prefer the dashed ascent's start (stroke-dasharray) since it is the trip start. Emit inside the .mv an empty aria-hidden <span id="route-start" class="mv-start" style="left:X%;top:Y%"> with X = x / m['w'] * 100 and Y = y / m['h'] * 100 (the viewBox equals w x h), position: absolute; width: 0; height: 0. Return from map_block whether the span was emitted and append #route-start to the expand link's href at core.py:524 only then, so the series page keeps a bare link and build.py --check keeps passing. In base.css give the span scroll-margin-right: 50vw and scroll-margin-top: calc((100svh - 140px) / 2) (fragment scrolling aligns block:start and inline:nearest; .fs-map's max-height is calc(100svh - 140px), base.css:374), so the start lands mid-screen. base.js:428-459 then only refines to the bounding box and adds is-pannable, and should skip faded and descent paths the same way. Show the 'Drag to pan' hint without JavaScript: every fs map is wider than any phone that can reach the page, so make .fs-pan visible by default and let base.js remove it only when scrollWidth <= clientWidth (base.css:372-373). Do not touch render.py or meta.json. Tests in tests/test_report_page.py (which already covers trips/<slug>/map/ at 1300-1311 and 3197-3231): the span's X and Y within 0-100 on a rendered single-day report, a ski report's span uses the ascent start, a multi-day overview takes the first non-faded path, the series page has no span and a bare link, the link carries #route-start exactly when the span exists. Fragment scrolling of a nested scroller cannot be exercised by the harnesses: check it in iOS Safari and Android Chrome (item 13).
   Files: tools/sitegen/core.py, assets/css/base.css, assets/js/base.js, tests/test_report_page.py.

In base.css beside :351: .map-full { display: none; } @media (min-width: 560px) and (max-width: 1199.98px) { .map-full { display: inline-flex; } }. Keep core.map_caption untouched (core.py:717). Extend tests/test_report_page.py (the fs href assertion at :2633 and the track-less case at :2507-2510, which must still find no map/ link) and tests/test_series_page.py:1197.

The key row stays in .fs-foot. The MI|KM classes keep the toggle working. One test that the bar holds both scale groups and the disclaimer.

13. The real-phone look (Eric's five minutes, scheduled by the session): the Back gesture closing the viewer (item 7), the swipe with drift (item 8), and the full-screen map opening on the route with JavaScript off (item 11) on an iPhone in Safari and, if one is at hand, an Android phone in Chrome. Write the script for him (which page, what to tap) and log his answer; docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 15.5 lists a real phone as never run.

What not to touch: content/ and rendered/ (no render; the route-start span is parsed from the committed overlay at build time); the journal's square sheet for track-only days; the viewer's keyboard behavior and focus return; the desktop copy-link path; render.py and meta.json.

Acceptance gate (run all of it and put the numbers in the report):
- Journal page: written days render core.photo_rows inside .ser-body with .ser-body .jrows capped at 620 px and --jmax/--jmin overrides, the three track-only days keep the square sheet, tests/test_series_page.py:900 and :911 updated; journal image bytes on the phone profile measured in the default write-ups-only view before and after and recorded in the log.
- July and August are pressable in the default view: no .ser-mnav link carries aria-disabled or tabindex after apply(), a press reveals the month and #ser-live announces 'Showing all 150 days'; the Node test asserts it.
- written_nav shows 'WRITE-UP 12 OF 32'; month heads and By-month rows show 'N MI SO FAR' and the last month's figure equals the strip's total (test); the day rows' 'So far' cell sums day files in order and gap rows do not change it.
- Continue reading: tests/series_js_harness.js gains a localStorage stub and cases for store-on-open, show-on-return, hidden-when-absent and a private-mode throw; nothing renders without JavaScript or a stored entry.
- Viewer: harness cases for pushState on open, popstate closing, Escape not over-popping, and the error state ('This photo could not be loaded' with the file link); Chrome touch emulation shows a swipe with vertical drift changes the photo, the page behind does not scroll, pinch still zooms, buttons still answer; opening announces 'Photo 1 of N. <alt>' and the dialog has aria-describedby="lb-live".
- Share: under (hover: none) the control reads 'Share this page' and calls navigator.share inside the click with the clipboard path as fallback; the desktop copy-link path is unchanged (harness).
- Full-screen map: a stdlib script over the 157 built report map/ pages finds the #route-start span inside the viewBox on every map with a .mk-trk and none on the series page (whose link stays bare); the ski ascent leg's start is used on ski maps; sitecheck passes with the #route-start fragments; the fs bar carries the u-mi/u-km scale groups and 'Not for navigation'; 'Open the full map' appears after the key rows at 560-1199 px only (cascade test) and the track-less fixture still has no map/ link.
- PCT landing's first action is btn btn--ink; the Back gesture, the swipe and the no-JS map opening were looked at on a real phone once and the result logged (15.5).
- Both Pythons with -W error and build.py --out <scratch> --link --check pass.

Screenshots: the PCT page at 1280 and 390 px before and after (Day 1 and Day 15 entries); a 1024 px report map section showing 'Open the full map'; the fs page at 390 px with the new bar; the viewer's error state. Keep them in .cache/plan2/phase11/.

Log entry: 'Phase 11 - The PCT journal, the photo viewer and the full-screen map'. Under author inputs: the sizes-cap decision made, the real-phone results or 'pending'.
```

**Gate:** the journal's written days render justified rows and the three track-only days keep the sheet, with the phone-profile image bytes recorded; July and August open from the bar and the announcement fires; 'WRITE-UP n OF 32' and 'N MI SO FAR' match the loader; the viewer's harness cases (history, error, swipe gate) pass and touch emulation scrolls nothing behind the dialog; every report `map/` page with a `.mk-trk` has `#route-start` inside the viewBox and the series page's link stays bare; `build.py --check` passes with the fragments; both Pythons pass with `-W error`; the real-phone look is done or logged as pending.

## Phase 12 - Maps on pages: landing on a trip, legends, touch, sticky columns

Lets a report link to its own row on the region map and the PCT to the maps that draw its days, makes cluster taps and legend toggles answer on `map/` and region pages, gives touch devices a select-then-open gesture on pins and lines, folds the 168-row list on phones, gives phone region maps a full-screen page, and keeps the stuck region map, ski panel, chapter rail and month bar inside common laptop and landscape-phone windows with one documented rule. The modules are `tools/sitegen/pages/archive.py`, `region.py`, `section.py`, `assets/js/base.js`, `archive.js`, `section.js`, and the page stylesheets. No hard prerequisite; the previews come before the three layout choices.

### Prompt

```
PHASE 12: Maps on pages: landing on a trip, legends, touch, sticky columns.

Goal: landing on one trip's row from its report; legends that filter; cluster presses that select; touch that previews before it navigates; a foldable phone list; full-screen region maps; sticky parts that fit a laptop window, with one rule and one test table.

Decisions to ask Eric (one message at the start for d and e; b, c and the 1005 px cap only after he has seen the side-by-side pictures the session builds):
(a) gap1-01: raise the 1000-1199 report map column to 1005 px and hang the stats strip from the column, after a preview at 1024 and 1180 against today's 860 (each can be taken or left).
(b) qa-04: let the ski season band dim the region map, after a side-by-side of 'All' beside a season.
(c) gap2-05: release the journal's month bar under 480 px of height, as a consequence of the one sticky rule (recommended yes).
(d) gap4-03: 'See on the <Region> map' also as a fourth title action at 1000 px and up (recommended no: the Route section and the goal pager are enough).
(e) qa-06: full-screen pages for the eight region maps (recommended yes).

Order: the ids and the landing (1-4), the links between pages (5-7), cluster, legend and touch (8-10), the phone map page (11-13), the sticky parts (14-20), then the report column, the ski dim and the two small CSS items (21-23).

1. gap4-01. Give every region-list row a stable id.
   Observation: rows in a region's 'In this region' list carry data-key but no id (archive.py:138-140 for reports, :201-203 for journal parts); the built Lake Tahoe page has 89 li.arc-row and none with an id; the map's pins have no id either (mapkit.py:1531), and any id inside the inlined SVG is rewritten per variant by core.frag (core.py:450-462). archive.js already opens the tab that contains any hash target (:37-41) and scrolls to it after load (:64-68), but folds rows 9 and on behind 'Show all N' afterwards (:71-85; archive.css:77 hides .arc-more), so a link to one of the 79 folded Lake Tahoe rows would land on a hidden row. A part's key is unique within one region only: the Lake Tahoe and Eastern Sierra parts share the key pacific-crest-trail-2024:pacific-crest-trail (content/trips/pacific-crest-trail-2024/index.md:18, :21) and map/ lists both rows (archive.py:315), so a region-less journal id would fail DUPLICATE IDS. tests/test_region_days.py:874-875 finds journal rows by the prefix '<li class="arc-row arc-row--journal"'.
   Proposal: in archive.py add id="arc-row-<slug>" to region_row (a trip sits in one region's rows; other_places reuses region_row, so unmapped regions' rows get ids too) and id="arc-row-<region slug>-<content.slugify(part['key'])>" to journal_row, passing the region's slug in from region_panel; put the id after data-key. Keep the prefix distinct from 'arc-rows-<region>'. In archive.js, after the fold loop, if the hash target is an .arc-row.arc-more inside a folded ol, press that ol's 'Show all' button as the cluster click does (base.js:188-198), scrollIntoView({block: 'center'}) and focus the row's link with preventScroll (the focusin handler at base.js:168-171 then highlights its pin or track and the row). sitecheck.duplicate_ids (:294) guards collisions and sitecheck.links (:242-279) verifies every cross-page '#arc-row-…' link. Update tests/test_region_page.py:1482-1496 (exact markup), tests/test_archive_page.py:258 (ids unique, fragments land), one maplist harness check (a hash to a folded row opens the list and focuses the row), and list the ids at docs/DEVELOPING.md:1119-1123.

2. gap4-02. Landing on a trip reuses the cluster-click sequence.
   Observation: base.js's highlight() and markRows() (:137-160) run only on hover and focus and the off handlers clear them (:162-172, :177-182); the cluster marker's click (:188-202) already opens a folded list, scrolls the row to center and focuses its link, after which focusin highlights the map and marks the row while focus stays.
   Proposal: nothing beyond item 1's fold-opening lines: a hash target that is an .arc-row goes through the same open-fold, scroll, focus sequence, and the existing focusin handler marks the trip on the map. No persistent pick state, no window global, no is-picked CSS, no Escape or hashchange handling. One sentence beside the map-list wiring in docs/DEVELOPING.md (:1092). For a trip whose start is in a cluster, .map.is-hl .mk-cluster.hl drops the marker to opacity .2 (base.css:364), so the trip shows by its track in route red; say so in the docs.

Leave fullscreen_page and share_row as they are; a fourth title action only if Eric said yes to (d). Document the helper under Region pages in docs/DEVELOPING.md (:981-986).

8. qa-02. A cluster click selects its reports.
   Observation: on map/ (Lake Tahoe) the '22' cluster near the Tallac trailheads stands for 22 trips; clicking it scrolls to the first matching row and focuses it (base.js:188-203); the is-map-hl marks on the other rows exist only while the pointer is over the cluster; 79 of the 86 rows sit behind 'Show all 86'; the cluster is an aria-hidden <g> (mapkit.py:1538) with no keyboard access. The review gate took unnamed clusters out of the Tab order (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:314) and tests/test_a11y_static.py rule D4 (marker_focus, :957-976) asserts base.js wires focus listeners to no .mk-cluster element; the harness stand-in clusters (tests/maplist_js_harness.js:224-225) carry aria-hidden too. A cluster's keys can include the planned route (known 15.4.7), so a count must count report rows. The home maps have no rows (base.js:183 guards on rowsFor(map).length).
   Proposal: in base.js, only for clusters that get .is-linked (maps with rows: map/ and regions/<slug>/, never the home maps), remove aria-hidden, add role="button", tabindex="0" and aria-label="N reports start here" (N from the rows the keys resolve to, not from data-keys), and turn click / Enter / Space into a sticky selection: keep is-map-hl on the cluster's rows, open the fold (the existing [aria-controls] press), add a new .is-off class (archive.css) to the panel's other rows, and write 'N reports start here · Show all' into the panel's count line (give the count_line(ts, 'ml--end') span built at archive.py:43 and placed at :240-247 an id). Clear the selection on Escape, on 'Show all' (archive.js), or on a second press of the same cluster, restoring the count line. Region pages share region_panel(page=True). Revise rule D4 (tests/test_a11y_static.py:957-976) to allow focus listeners on named clusters and update the stand-in clusters in the harness; extend the cluster cases (lines 12-13, 224-225) with select, second-press clear, Escape clear, keyboard and the aria-hidden removal. The cluster markup comes from rendered/site, so every attribute change is at runtime; no render.
   Files: assets/js/base.js, assets/js/pages/archive.js, assets/css/pages/archive.css, tools/sitegen/pages/archive.py, tests/maplist_js_harness.js, tests/test_a11y_static.py.

10. gap1-03. On touch screens, a first tap on a pin or line selects it; a second tap opens it.
   Observation: everything that names or highlights a map marker runs on mouseenter/mouseleave/focusin/focusout (base.js:172-187); a pin and a journal line are <a> elements (mapkit.py:1531, :644-647), so on a touch screen a tap navigates at once and the visitor never learns which trip a pin is; (pointer: coarse) appears nowhere in CSS or JS (the only touch rule is section.css:240) and the log records 'there is no hover on touch' (docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md:1076); the journal line's hit stroke is 14 px (LINE_HIT, mapkit.py:633). tests/maplist_js_harness.js builds its own vm sandbox (lines 192-200) with no matchMedia; tests/page_standin.js serves only the trips and ticklist harnesses.
   Proposal: in base.js, inside the pin/line loop at :174, when window.matchMedia && matchMedia('(hover: none)').matches and rowsFor(map).length is non-zero, add a click listener on .mk-pin[data-key] and .mk-line[data-key]: if the element is not the current selection, preventDefault(), call highlight(map, keys) and markRows(map, keys), mark it is-sel (clearing any other), and reuse the cluster-click body (open a folded list, scrollIntoView block center, focus the row's link) so the marked row names the trip. A second tap on the selected marker navigates; a tap on the map paper clears. Maps without rows (home.py:323-330) are left as they are. In base.css under @media (pointer: coarse): .mk-line .mk-hit { stroke-width: 28px !important; } (pins lie above lines and still answer first, DEVELOPING.md:1184). Gate on (hover: none), never on width. Tests: add a matchMedia stub to the maplist harness sandbox (returning {matches: false} by default and {matches: true} for '(hover: none)' in a coarse-pointer run) and a run with tap, tap again, tap elsewhere, a map without rows. No Python change.
   Files: assets/js/base.js, assets/css/base.css, tests/maplist_js_harness.js.

14. gap2-07. One documented rule and one test table for every sticky part (write this first among the sticky items; the others fill it in).
   Observation: the site has eight sticky parts with five different height policies: the header (base.css:150, static only without JS under 480 px wide), .arc-mapcol (archive.css:41, no guard), .sec-panel-in (section.css:265, scaled to 100vh, static under 600 px), .abo-nums (about.css:14, sticks only at min-height 880), .mul-dnav (multiday.css:29/34, static under 480 px), .mul-rail (multiday.css:246, no guard), .rep-rail (report.css:86, no guard, harmless: the tallest Beta rail has 3 rows), .ser-mnav (series.css:107, no guard). docs/DEVELOPING.md:140-146 documents only the second-bar scroll margins and the day bar's 480 px rule; tests/test_a11y_static.py's ShortViewportTests (:2730-2755) cover the day bar and pin the month bar. The static CSS evaluator there (computed(node, prop, rules, env(width, height))) can evaluate any of these at 1366x683 or 1280x620 without a browser, but does not compute calc(), so the tests assert declarations, not pixels.
   Proposal: a 'Sticky parts' table in docs/DEVELOPING.md (element, sticks from which width, top value, what it holds and how tall, the guard or fit rule, the test) and the rule stated once: a stuck element fits header + 24 + its content inside 100svh on a 683 px viewport or scales or releases, with 480 px (bars) and 600 px (columns) as the two release heights. Extend ShortViewportTests with one subTest table over (1366,683), (1280,620), (1152,630), (1440,790), (667,375) for .arc-mapcol, .sec-panel-in, .abo-nums, .mul-dnav, .mul-rail, .ser-mnav and .p-fsmap .fs-map's max-height, asserting position and, for the fitted ones, that the width declaration contains '100svh'. A browser check at 1366x768 and 1280x720 remains the final word.
   Files: docs/DEVELOPING.md, tests/test_a11y_static.py.

15. gap2-01. Fit the stuck region map to the viewport on map/ and region pages.
   Observation: archive.css:39-43 makes .arc-mapcol sticky at 1000 px and up with top: calc(var(--header-h) + 24px) and no height guard; the column holds the 718x620 desktop render plus a two-line caption (six items, 12 + 2x20 = 52 px). At 1366x768 (683 px viewport) the map is 718x620, stuck from y=96 to 716, so the bottom 33 px (the compact scale bar's '10 mi' label sits at y=591 of 620) and the whole caption are off-screen; at 1280x720 the map is 681x588 with 64 px cut plus the caption; only at 1024x768 does the map fit. A visitor reaches that state after about 204 px of scrolling and stays in it for the whole opened Lake Tahoe list (89 rows, about 5,400 px). The ski panel already solves the same problem by scaling its map to 100vh and going static under 600 px (section.css:275-284). Region pages reuse the panel (region.py:181). The first-round captures are full-page, so no one has seen this stuck state.
   Proposal: model the fix on section.css:277-284. In archive.css at 1000 px and up: .arc-mapv .mv { width: min(100%, calc((100svh - var(--header-h) - 24px - 52px - 12px) * 718 / 620)); } (52 px is the two-line caption; one budget serves both bands; if the browser check shows the credit line wrapping at 1000-1199, add a band override with 72 px); the stuck column is then header + 24 + map + caption + 12 to spare: 606 px wide (0.84x) at 683 px, 556 (0.77x) at 640. Add @media (min-width: 1000px) and (max-height: 599.98px) { .arc-mapcol { position: static; } .arc-mapv .mv { width: 100%; } }, the same release as the ski panel. The inline aspect-ratio on .mv keeps the height in step. Extend ShortViewportTests with .arc-mapcol at (1366,683) sticky + width containing '100svh', and (1300,599) static. Check in a browser at 1366x768 and 1280x720 that the scale bar and caption stay visible while the list scrolls (captures).
   Files: assets/css/pages/archive.css, tests/test_a11y_static.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

Below 1000 the grid stacks .arc-mapcol above .arc-side, so the legend already follows the caption in reading order. tests/test_build_site.py:1510-1520 and tests/test_region_page.py:1434-1441 pin the legend inside .arc-side before .arc-inreg.

Keep the forced-colors rule (archive.css:58) and the two aria-labeled lists; no second element, no JS condition. Update the two pinning tests and add the placement to tests/test_archive_page.py.

17. gap2-02. Ski page: show the phone-shaped panel render on short desktop windows.

21. gap1-01. The 1000-1199 report map column and the day-map alignment.
   Observation: at 560-1199 the map, key rows, caption and profile share a column of min(860px, 100% − 2·40px) hung from the left margin (report.css:23-26; --col-max base.css:18; --m 40 from 760, base.css:21): 688 px at 768, 754 at 834, 860 from 940 up, so at 1024 / 1100 / 1180 the wrap is 944 / 1020 / 1100 px and 84 / 160 / 240 px of paper sit to the map's right while the stats strip above it (a full .wrap, report.py:613) runs the whole width; the 1440 px wide render is hidden there (base.css:338-339 shows only mv--col at 560-1199). The multi-day day maps stop at 718 px between 760 and 999 (multiday.css:241) while the overview above them runs to 860, so the two maps on one page have different right edges from 800 px up. rep-strip is also emitted by the series page (series.py:169), whose map sits in the 5fr/6fr intro grid, not the 860 column. The col render's hillshade is drawn at 2x (render.py:239), so a 1.4x cap (1005 px) changes label size (11 px to about 15 px) and stroke weight only; report.css:21-22 states 'the 718 renders never scale past 1.2x'.
   Proposal: (1) no-regret: in multiday.css:241 change .mul-dmap-w, .mul-dp { max-width: 718px; } to max-width: var(--col-max) so the day map and day profile share the overview's right edge at 760-999 (860/718 = 1.198, inside the rule). (2) Eric's choice after one side-by-side preview at 1024 and 1180 (today's 860 beside the alternative): (a) hang the stats strip from the map column: body:not(.p-series) .rep-strip { width: min(var(--col-max), 100% - 2 * var(--m)); margin-inline: var(--m) auto; } in the 560-1199 block of report.css (page CSS is bundled after base.css, so it overrides .wrap at equal specificity); (b) raise the cap for 1000-1199 to 1.4x of the 718 render: --col-max: 1005px under @media (min-width: 1000px) and (max-width: 1199.98px) in base.css; section.css:110 and report.css:25 take the new value through the variable. Each of (a) and (b) can be taken or left. If (b) is taken, rewrite the rule at report.css:21-22 and the breakpoint note at docs/DEVELOPING.md:69. The wide render is not an option below 1200.

22. qa-04. The ski season band dims the region map (only if Eric picked it from the preview).
   Observation: on ski/ choosing '2023–24' hides every other season's rows (4 reports shown, URL #season-2023-24; section.js apply() 38-62 toggles .is-off on [data-f] only) while the 'Ski map by region' panel still reads 'Lake Tahoe 37' and draws all 37 pins; highlight() (base.js:137-155) dims via .is-hl and is a closure whose off handlers clear to null; ski rows carry only data-key (section.py:233 via core.trip_row) and section.js:118-122 finds a row's region through the map's .mk-cat, so no tab-label counts.
   Proposal: give base.js a per-map base selection: highlight(map, keys) stays as is, but each map stores map._base (null by default) and every off handler calls highlight(map, map._base) instead of highlight(map, null); expose one function on window (e.g. window.MapHL = { setBase: function (map, keys) {…} }) that sets map._base and applies it. In section.js apply(), after the .is-off pass, collect the data-keys of the rows still visible and call setBase(maps, key === 'all' ? null : keys), so pins, clusters and tracks outside the season dim with the existing .is-hl styling (base.css:359-364) and a hover restores the season dim rather than clearing it. Build 'All' beside a season side by side at 1280 px and show Eric before deciding: the dimmed map must read as filtered, not broken. Harness case for season + hover composition.
   Files: assets/js/pages/section.js, assets/js/base.js, tests/maplist_js_harness.js.

23. a11y-06 and gap1-05. A CSS-only focus halo on journal lines; the Ski list's stat cells two by two at 960-1099.
   Observation (a11y-06): a journal part on a region map is a focusable link (a.mk-line) whose only focus indicator is the highlight base.js applies on focusin; base.css:357 sets .mk-line:focus-visible { outline: none } with the comment that base.js picks the lines out; without JavaScript, or before the deferred bundle runs, a keyboard user tabbing the Lake Tahoe map lands on an invisible stop (DEVELOPING.md:146-151 records the script highlight as the line's focus indicator). Each <use class="mk-hit"> carries stroke: transparent as an inline style in the committed fragment (mapkit.py:641-644), so a stylesheet rule needs !important or a render.
   Proposal (a11y-06): in base.css add .mk-line:focus-visible .mk-hit { stroke: var(--ink) !important; stroke-opacity: .35; } and, in the forced-colors block beside .mk-pin:focus-visible (base.css:743), .mk-line:focus-visible .mk-hit { stroke: Highlight !important; stroke-opacity: 1 }. That draws the 14 px hit stroke as a halo along the whole part with no script; base.js's highlight layers on top; keep opacity under .4 so pins over the line stay legible. Update DEVELOPING.md:149-150 and add an assertion in tests/test_a11y_static.py that the rule exists and carries !important. Dropping the inline stroke from mapkit.line_links waits for Phase 15's render.
   Observation (gap1-05): from 960 the Ski page splits into the list and a sticky 360 px panel (390 px from 1100; section.css:260-278, 286): the list is 496 px at 960 and 560 at 1024, the row text column 316/380 px after the 160 px tile (:266), and the four stat columns designed at 120 px (base.css:519) get 79/95 px, at or under a five-digit Data-M value with its unit at the 16 px size section.css:271 sets; from 1100 the 390 px panel leaves the columns 106-120 px.
   Proposal (gap1-05): a new query beside the 960-1199 block at section.css:269-278, @media (min-width: 960px) and (max-width: 1099.98px) { .sec-list .trow-stats { grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0, 120px)); row-gap: 8px; } }. Build the Ski page at 960, 1024 and 1100 before and after and show both before merging. Leave the sticky panel and the hover follow as they are.
   Files: assets/css/base.css, assets/css/pages/section.css, tests/test_a11y_static.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

What not to touch: rendered/ and mapkit (every attribute on a cluster, pin or line is added at build or run time; the inline stroke on .mk-hit waits for Phase 15); content/; the home maps' single-tap navigation; base.css:25's scroll-padding declaration; the region-map legend's forced-colors rule.

Acceptance gate (run all of it and put the numbers in the report):
- Every li.arc-row carries id arc-row-<slug>, journal rows arc-row-<region>-<part>; build.py --check reports no DUPLICATE IDS on map/ and no BROKEN ANCHOR for any 'See on the <Region> map' link on the 168 report pages; a completed-goal fixture gets the plain region link; the PCT intro lists its parts with label_range ranges and links that land.
- tests/maplist_js_harness.js: a hash to a folded row opens the list, scrolls and focuses it (reusing the cluster-click sequence); a cluster press selects its rows with .is-off on the rest and 'N reports start here · Show all' in the count line (N counts reports, not the planned route), Escape, Show all or a second press clears; aria-hidden removed and role=button added only on linked clusters (rule D4 revised); legend toggles filter rows and dim non-matching markers with forced-colors discs kept; coarse-pointer run with a matchMedia stub: tap selects, second tap opens, tap elsewhere clears, a map without rows is untouched; a focused journal line shows the CSS halo without base.js.
- Region pages show a Mono-S activity line with counts linking trips/?region=&activity=; the section heads link their region names; the place line links the region at every width (tests/test_report_page.py, both layouts) and the goal pager's region cell lands on the planned line's row.
- Phone captures at 390 px: map/ folds every year after the first behind the Show-all idiom and a year link or hash unfolds it; region maps carry the in-map credit tag and a compact key row (home maps unchanged or with .hom-attr hidden under 560); map/<region-slug>/ exists for the 8 regions (noindex, index=False) with the 44 px button on phone region maps, the fscol width and whole-map centering; region pages share it.
- ShortViewportTests in tests/test_a11y_static.py has a subTest table over (1366,683), (1280,620), (1152,630), (1440,790) and (667,375) for .arc-mapcol, .sec-panel-in, .abo-nums, .mul-dnav, .mul-rail, .ser-mnav and .p-fsmap .fs-map asserting position and a '100svh' width where fitted; DEVELOPING.md has the 'Sticky parts' table and the one rule (bars release at 480 px of height, columns at 600).
- Browser checks recorded with captures (in .cache/plan2/phase12/): at 1366x768 and 1280x720 the stuck Lake Tahoe map shows its scale bar and caption with a one-line key beside it while the 89-row list scrolls; the ski panel shows the phone render under 760 px of height with readable labels; the Haute Route rail's 'Day N GPX' button is visible at 683 px; a 667x375 landscape phone gets about 82 % of the full-screen map page as map; the journal's month bar releases under 480 px of height (or is recorded as the exception).
- Day maps and day profiles share the overview's right edge at 760-999 px (multiday.css max-width var(--col-max)); the Ski list's stat cells go two by two at 960-1099 px; the season band dims pins outside the chosen season only if Eric chose it from the preview; the 1005 px cap and the hung strip only if he chose them from the 1024/1180 preview.
- Both Pythons with -W error and build.py --out <scratch> --link --check pass; tests/test_region_page.py, test_archive_page.py and test_region_days.py updated for the ids.

Screenshots: the side-by-side sheets for gap1-01 (1024 and 1180), qa-04 ('All' beside a season) and gap1-05 (960/1024/1100); the browser checks above; map/ and regions/lake-tahoe/ at 390 px before and after; a report's Route section with 'See on the Lake Tahoe map'.

Log entry: 'Phase 12 - Maps on pages'. Under author inputs: the three preview choices and his answers, the cluster count rule (reports only), the rule D4 revision.
```

**Gate:** every `arc-row` id is unique on `map/` and every 'See on the … map' and PCT part link lands (`--check` clean); the maplist harness passes its new cases (folded-row landing, cluster selection, legend toggles, coarse-pointer taps, the CSS halo); `ShortViewportTests` covers the seven sticky parts at the five sizes and `DEVELOPING.md` has the table and the rule; the browser captures at 1366x768 and 1280x720 show the stuck map's scale bar and caption, the ski panel's phone render, and the rail's GPX button; `map/` folds its years at 390 px and `map/<region>/` exists for the eight regions; the three layout choices were made from side-by-side pictures or left; both Pythons pass with `-W error`.

## Phase 13 - Phone, tablet, accessibility and copy polish

A sweep of small CSS, markup and wording fixes in three sessions: 13A the header, menu and units switch; 13B legibility and touch (the 12 px floor, no stuck hover, text-zoom-safe chips, year heads, card tiles, section list headers, the print sheet); 13C accessibility and copy (the home target size, heading order, forced colors, spoken units, contrast mode, the meta-line dot, consistent labels), ending with the tablet capture profile and Eric's first VoiceOver pass. Each session pastes the Standing instructions and then its own prompt block below (13A, 13B or 13C); each block repeats its decisions, its what-not-to-touch list, its acceptance list and its log-entry name. One log entry per session (13A, 13B, 13C). visual-01 step 2 (a 400 px card tile) waits for Phase 15.

### Prompt 13A

```
PHASE 13A: Phone, tablet, accessibility and copy polish, session 1 of 3: header, menu and units switch. Paste this block alone after the Standing instructions; the other two sessions have their own blocks. The session ends with its own log entry and report.

Decisions to ask Eric at the start (one message; the recommended answers stand if he does not reply):
(a) The units switch on phones: gap6-02 (the Units row moved to the top of the phone menu; keeps plan 8.1; recommended) or gap6-01 (the MI|KM control beside the menu button at every width; reverses 8.1).
(b) gap6-03: start in kilometers when navigator.language does not start with 'en' (recommended yes).

   Observation: the main nav appears only from 1140 px (base.css:171), but the two-column desktop grids start at 1000 px (report.css:84-88, about.css:6-11, archive.css:39-43, the footer base.css:246), so an iPad in landscape (1024-1180 px) gets desktop layouts under a hamburger header. The row fits at 1000: the header row is 920 px (base.css:151, --m 40 at :21); the brand column is about 210 px (its mono caption .brand-c, 12 px, shown from 760, base.css:155/170), five 15 px links with 28 px gaps about 310, separator and gaps about 57, Search about 72 (Phase 7's box is wider: check it), units about 90; roughly 790 px. base.js:133 closes the menu at innerWidth >= 1140; tests/test_a11y_static.py:2453-2454, the NS_WIDTHS pair at 2493-2494 and line 2630 pin 1139/1140; DEVELOPING.md:70 states 1140.
   Proposal: @media (min-width: 1000px) { .nav { display: flex; } .menu-btn { display: none; } }, keeping the 28 px gaps (20 px at 1000-1139 only if a browser check shows the row tight with Phase 7's search box; hide .brand-c below 1140 if still tight); change base.js:133's 1140 to 1000; update the three pinned widths and DEVELOPING.md:70. Check 1000, 1024 and 1139 with and without scripting (the no-JS wrap rules, base.css:177-183).
   Files: assets/css/base.css, assets/js/base.js, tests/test_a11y_static.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

2. phone-12. A Home row in the phone menu.
   Observation: below 1140 px (1000 after item 1) the brand name is the only link home; on trip pages below 760 px, once the H1 has scrolled under, body.past-title hides the brand (base.css:195-196) and the app bar in its place links to #title (core.py:1669), so getting home takes two taps or the footer; the menu's Site column (core.py:1660-1666) lists Trips, Tick list, Map & archive, PCT, About and Search (a form after Phase 7), never Home; tools/lib/icons.py has no 'home' glyph.
   Proposal: prepend a 'Home' row to site_rows in core.header (a 'home' key that cur() marks aria-current="page" on the home page) with a 'home' path drawn in tools/lib/icons.py; update the menu-row assertions in tests/test_nav.py:72, 120, 131-132 and tests/test_ticklist_page.py:1254, 1275 (there is no tests/test_core_header.py).

3. phone-13. A title bar with back-to-top on the long index pages.
   Observation: the phone app bar (route glyph + title, tap = back to #title) is emitted only when core.header gets a trip (core.py:1667-1670) and base.js observes the H1 only when .ab-trip exists (base.js:279-285); report, multi-day and series pages have it (the PCT page included). Trips (about 31 screens of 844 px), Map & archive (about 20), the Ski section (about 12), the region pages and the tick list have no in-page way back to the top, the search box or the region tabs; their only fragment link is the #main skip link. The H1 ids already exist in the build (#trips-title, #arc-title, #sec-title, #reg-title, #tkl-title); About has no H1 id and is about 3.3 screens, the goal page about 2.2. Known: docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 15.4.8 (the Trips search box starts below the fold on a phone).
   Proposal: generalize the app bar: core.document already has the page title and calls header(here, active, trip); let header take page_title and page_top (the H1 id) and emit a.ab-page (title + up-arrow icon, href to that id) when there is no trip, for Trips, Map, the section, region and tick-list pages; decide per page module which pages get the bar (About, 404 and the goal page do not). base.js observes that heading (data-ab-title) with the same 52 px root margin and toggles body.past-title. On Trips point the link at #search (the form is hidden until trips.js runs; the bar needs JS anyway). Ship with item 2, since the brand hides while past the title. Update tests/test_nav.py and the page tests that assert header markup.

4. gap6-02. The Units row above the fold of the open phone menu (if Eric chose it).
   Observation: in the #menu panel the Units row comes after the Reports list (5 rows) and the Site list (6 rows, 7 after item 2): with rows of min-height 64 px plus a 1 px rule (base.css:217-218), the panel's 16 px top padding (:212-213), two labels, two list rules and the row's own 24 px margin (:224), the control's top sits about 833 px below the header in a panel that is 788 px tall on a 390x844 phone, so a phone reader who opens the menu to find units has to scroll it. Plan 8.1 (docs/personal-website-improvement-plan.md:259) asks that the switch not get more visual emphasis than the main destinations. .menu nav declares only grid-template-columns (base.css:227); base.js:124/130 focuses the first link on open.
   Proposal: emit .menu-units first inside .menu nav on phones as a compact 44 px row ('Units' t-label + the 32 px control, no list styling, its own 1 px rule), visually quieter than the Reports and Site rows; from 760 px keep the two-column layout by placing it explicitly: .menu-c1 { grid-column: 1; grid-row: 1 } .menu-c2 { grid-column: 2; grid-row: 1 } .menu-units { grid-column: 2; grid-row: 2 }. Focus handling is unchanged. Update tests/test_nav.py:133-135 (the row now precedes .menu-c1) and tests/test_a11y_static.py:2609. If Eric chose gap6-01 instead: show the MI|KM control beside the menu button at every width and remove .menu-units and the duplicate control.
   Files: tools/sitegen/core.py, assets/css/base.css, tests/test_nav.py, tests/test_a11y_static.py.

5. gap6-03. Start in kilometers when the browser's language is not English (if Eric said yes).
   Observation: the pre-paint script only honors a stored choice (try{if(localStorage.getItem("units")==="km")…}, core.py:1745) and base.js:20 reads that class on load, so every first-time reader starts in miles and feet; About's first units sentence (about.py:127) is pinned as true without JavaScript by tests/test_about_units.py, and the JS-only .abo-units-how span is hidden without scripting (about.css:40). tests/test_sitecheck.py:763 is a fixture that quotes a sample script, not a pin on the real one.
   Proposal: extend the pre-paint script: when no 'units' value is stored and navigator.language is set and does not start with 'en' (case-insensitive), add the km class; base.js:20 then shows the control in the right state and a click still stores the choice. English of any region stays miles; the no-JS case stays miles (base.css:47-49). Keep About's first sentence and extend the .abo-units-how span: 'Browsers set to a language other than English start in kilometers and meters. The MI | KM switch changes every page, and this browser remembers the choice.' Update tests/test_about_units.py (SWITCH constant) and DEVELOPING.md's units rule. navigator.language is read locally; nothing leaves the browser.
   Files: tools/sitegen/core.py, assets/js/base.js, tools/sitegen/pages/about.py, tests/test_about_units.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

6. phone-10. Short and sideways phones: reclaim the fixed chrome.
   Observation: a report keeps 56 px of header (base.css:17, 150) and a 65 px bottom bar (report.css:185-187: border + 4 + 56 + 4; the footer reserves 72) on screen, 121 px; on a 667x375 phone that is a third of the height, which the review rated and left (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 15.4 point 12). The viewport meta (core.py:1734) has no viewport-fit=cover, so env(safe-area-inset-bottom) is 0: leave it that way (cover would expose every bleeding element to the notch and cannot be verified without a real iPhone).
```

```
23. a11y-15. A prefers-contrast: more block.
   Observation: measured from the tokens at base.css:5-10: --ink-2 on paper 8.22:1, --ink-3 on paper 4.93, on surface 5.33, on sunk 4.47, on profile-fill 4.18; --rule-strong borders 3.05. The chart and map labels are not hex literals inside the rendered SVGs: the fragments carry classes only and their rules (.pf-ax, .mk-lbl, fill #66686D, mapkit.py:59, :68) come from mapkit.MAP_CSS, which build.py:81-85 bundles into site.css, so page CSS overrides them with no render; the glyph/tile SVGs served as <img> (build.py:120-134 inlines their rules) and the key-row symbols with inline hex at core.KEY_SYMBOLS (core.py:649-655) cannot follow page CSS. --route colors only the nav/tab accent bars (the track's red is substituted at build time, build.py:128), so leave it.
   Proposal: add to base.css @media (prefers-contrast: more) { :root { --ink-3: var(--ink-2); --rule-strong: #5F5D57; } .pf-ax, .mk-lbl { fill: var(--ink-2); } } and state the two limits in DEVELOPING.md (the <img> glyphs and the inline key-row symbols). Separately verify that no ink-3 text sits on a --sunk background in the default palette (multiday.css:207 .mul-xfer is the one to check) and fix that if it does.

24. qa-01. Silence the generated meta-line dot for screen readers.
   Observation: every .ml meta line draws its separator with .ml > .mi::before { content: "·" } (base.css:101) and also carries a visually hidden ', ' per item (core.py:275); Chrome exposes the generated dot in the accessibility tree, so the home card links are described as '· SAT, JAN 17, 2026 · , SAWTOOTH RIDGE' and the Trips head reads '· 167 REPORTS · , 15 REGIONS · , 2021–2026' (.cache/plan2/qa/snap-trips-filtered.txt lines 25-30). .ml--end > .mi::before (base.css:108) only repositions the dot and sets no content; base.css:643 (.rtab--compact …::before, dead after item 16) had a third dot; archive.css:174-176 already uses the two-declaration fallback pattern.
   Proposal: change base.css:101 to .ml > .mi::before { content: "·"; content: "·" / ""; position: absolute; left: calc(var(--ml-sep) / 3); } (the first declaration is the fallback for engines without the slash syntax: Chrome 77+, Safari 17.4+, Firefox 128+ support it). The sr-only comma is then the only separator read. Re-check with an accessibility snapshot of the home page and trips/: no StaticText '·' inside .ml lines.
   Files: assets/css/base.css.
```
