**Gate:** 13A: the header nav shows from 1000 px, the menu has Home first and Units above the lists, the long index pages carry the title bar, and a blocked `site.js` still gives the no-JS header in Chrome. 13B: no 11 px rule applies at 390 px, every decorative `:hover` is guarded and touch emulation shows no stuck highlight, the year heads and card tiles look as captured, the print preview was checked in two browsers. 13C: built-home Lighthouse accessibility is 100 and the Matterhorn report stays 100/100/100, the home snapshot has no '·' text, one word per destination across header, menu, footer and 404, the screen-reader script exists and Eric's run is logged or pending, the tablet captures exist. Every session: both Pythons pass with `-W error` and the log entry names its checks.

## Phase 14 - Loading, metadata, sharing, follow and contact

Makes each report an honest search result and share card (activity and place in the title, whole descriptions, `og:site_name` and breadcrumbs), closes the ten-minute stale-stylesheet window, self-hosts the fonts if Eric agrees, fixes the two measured image misses, gives readers a Follow section and a way to reach Eric, and sets up Search Console and the first real share preview. Needs Phase 7 (`markdown.plain_sentences`). Most of the contact items wait on decisions only Eric can make.

### Prompt

```
PHASE 14: Loading, metadata, sharing, follow and contact.

Goal: honest search results and share cards; no stale stylesheet after a deploy; self-hosted fonts; two image fixes; a Follow section and a contact path; Search Console and a real share preview.

Decisions to ask Eric at the start (one message; items 15-20 are built only on his answers):
(a) perf-02: self-host the four Latin woff2 files under assets/fonts/ with display: swap and two preloads, measured before and after (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 12.3; recommended yes); 'optional' only after a later measurement.
(b) Contact path (gap5-01): a dedicated public alias as a plain mailto on About and in the footer, with 'Ask about this trip' on every report (recommended), an obfuscated address (not recommended), a hosted form or newsletter provider (the site's first third-party endpoint), or none. The address stays in git history for good (docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md section 6), so it should be an alias he is willing to keep public. He must supply the alias himself; nothing is guessed.
(c) Profile links (gap5-03): only the services and handles he names (recommended: at most the Strava profile if he wants one; nothing is guessed).
(d) Search Console (seo-12): set up a verified property and submit sitemap.xml (recommended yes; his Google account work); the esichak.github.io user-site repository for a root robots.txt is optional (recommended skip).
(e) Share image for the 62 photo-less reports (seo-04; docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 12.4): keep the text card unless the real preview check (item 10) shows a defect.

Order: titles, descriptions and metadata (1-4), asset versioning and fonts (5-6), the two image fixes (7-8), the share tools and checks (9-11), the feed, the icon and Search Console (12-14), then contact and follow (15-20).

1. seo-01. Put the activity and place into every trip page's title tag.
   Observation: every trip page's <title> and og:title are '{title} · Eric Sichak' (core.py:1723), with the date added only when a title repeats (report.py:649-650; 12 repeated titles, 'Ride near Day Valley' seven times, 'Tallac' four); titles such as 'Good fun · Eric Sichak' or 'AIARE 2 · Eric Sichak' tell a searcher nothing about the page. The H1 is verbatim, so the title tag is the only place facts can go. About 33 of the 167 published titles already contain their own place or region string (the plan's seo-01 count). 79 trips have no place; every trip has a region. tests/test_metadata.py:1640-1645 pins the exact form on three trip pages and 1647-1659 is the twin-title test; DEVELOPING.md has no title note today.
   Proposal: core.page_title(t, dup) used for full_title in report.py (single-day at :661 and goal at :566), multiday.py:797 and series.py:527: '{title} · {core.act_word(t)}, {place or region}' plus ', {short date}' when dup, then ' · Eric Sichak'; leave out a place or region whose words already appear in the title; fall back to today's form when place and region are both absent or both skipped. Activity, place and region come from index.md only. og:title takes the same string; the H1 and the BlogPosting headline stay the title as written. Home, section and region titles unchanged. Update both tests; add the rule beside the share-metadata notes in DEVELOPING.md:736-752 and CONTENT_GUIDE §9 (:896), and the comment at report.py:647-648.
   Files: tools/sitegen/core.py, tools/sitegen/pages/report.py, tools/sitegen/pages/multiday.py, tools/sitegen/pages/series.py, tests/test_metadata.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md, docs/CONTENT_GUIDE.md.

2. seo-02. Stop meta descriptions ending mid-sentence.
   Observation: report.py:651-654 clips 'summary + excerpt' to 155 characters with core.clip_words (core.py:307-312), so 118 of the 168 trip pages end in '…', often inside the facts (back-to-clipless ends 'with map, elevation…'; the Matterhorn's ends 'had me pretty…'). multiday.description (multiday.py:766-775) already writes the summary whole and adds 'Day N: …' only when it fits. core.summary() runs 116-220 characters on single-day reports (median 166), so a 160 limit cannot hold it; 100 of the 156 single-day pages are facts-led (track-only, a write-up under 100 characters, or a repeated title). build.feed uses summary() (build.py:225-240).
   Proposal: core.describe(t, limit=160) used on single-day and goal pages (report.py:651-654 and :563) and replacing multiday.description: when the page is facts-led write core.summary(t) whole regardless of limit, with the excerpt appended only when its first sentence fits in the room left; when a write-up leads, cut at the last sentence end between 80 and 160 characters with Phase 7's markdown.plain_sentences, and only then fall back to the word cut with '…'. Keep summary() unchanged. Tests in tests/test_metadata.py for the three shapes (test_the_description_is_the_factual_summary_then_the_start_of_the_write_up at :1956 changes).
   Files: tools/sitegen/core.py, tools/sitegen/pages/report.py, tools/sitegen/pages/multiday.py, tests/test_metadata.py.

5. perf-01. A version query on the bundle addresses.
   Observation: every page links assets/site.css and assets/site.js by bare name (core.py:1740-1747) and GitHub Pages sends cache-control: max-age=600 with an ETag on both (live headers, 2026-10-13), so a visitor who loaded the stylesheet within ten minutes before a deploy receives new HTML with the old stylesheet (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:862-863). A hashed file name would 404 for a cached old page once build() has emptied _site/ (build.py:273-275); a query string has none of that: the CDN serves assets/site.css?v=x with the same 200 / ETag / max-age=600 headers (curl 2026-10-13), sitecheck strips '?' (sitecheck.py:263-267), and the 404 page goes through core.document (notfound.py:44). 43 lines in 9 files name the bundles.
   Proposal: in build.py build the two bundles before the page loop, take the first 8 hex of SHA-1 over each bundle's bytes and set core.ASSET_VERSION = {'css': h1, 'js': h2} (default {} so document() called directly in tests still emits the bare names); core.document() appends ?v=<hash> to assets/site.css and assets/site.js when set. After the next deploy, curl -I the live versioned asset: 200 with max-age=600.

6. perf-02. Self-host the three fonts and preload the two above the fold (if Eric said yes).
   Observation: every page loads a render-blocking stylesheet from fonts.googleapis.com (core.py:32-33, 1742) which, fetched as Chrome, is 8,416 bytes with 21 @font-face blocks; the Latin files it names weigh 90,096 B (Archivo variable), 48,704 B (Source Serif 4 regular), 50,560 B (Source Serif 4 italic) and 23,108 B (Geist Mono), 212 KB in all, from a second origin. The report measured that on desktop first paint followed that stylesheet's arrival by 39-89 ms in all 25 runs, and the stylesheet itself arrived 236-347 ms after navigation start (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:141-147). The build copies only top-level assets/*.* (build.py:300-303). All three faces are SIL OFL.
   Proposal: tools/fonts.py (stdlib urllib, run once on the Mac with internet) that downloads the four Latin woff2 files Google serves today into assets/fonts/ with the three OFL license texts, and writes assets/css/fonts.css holding four @font-face rules (font-display: swap, the same wdth/wght/ital ranges as the Google URL, unicode-range for Latin). Bundle fonts.css first in css_bundle(); extend the asset copy in build.py to assets/fonts/; in core.document() drop the Google stylesheet and the two preconnect links and emit <link rel="preload" as="font" type="font/woff2" crossorigin> for Archivo and Source Serif 4 regular only (italic and mono load on demand). Add a test that every @font-face src names a copied file. Measure desktop first paint and phone LCP with the DEVELOPING.md:1428 method before and after (25 desktop runs as the report did); expect first paint to move to the arrival of site.css. 'optional' on the Tick list only after that measurement, as a separate decision.

Log entry: 'Phase 14 - Loading, metadata, sharing, follow and contact'. Under author inputs: the alias, the profile links, the Search Console steps, the Rich Results Test, the W3C feed validation, the curl check and the share preview, each done or pending.
```

**Gate:** every trip page's title carries the activity and place with no duplicate indexable titles; no description ends in '…' when facts lead; `share_preview.py` runs and its flag counts are in the log; `og:site_name`, `og:locale` and one BreadcrumbList per crumbed page pass `--check`; the bundles carry `?v=<hash>`; the fonts are self-hosted with the before/after first-paint figures recorded, or Eric declined; the ski preload and the hiking `.th` srcset are in the build; the contact items render nothing without `content/author.json` and everything with Eric's values; both Pythons pass with `-W error`; the after-deploy checks (curl, Rich Results, W3C, the real share preview) are done or listed as pending in the log.

## Phase 15 - Renderer round and storage budget

Lands every surviving renderer change in one VERSION bump with a safety net built first (offline golden and contract tests, a before/after contact sheet, a resumable budgeted run, the renderer version recorded in `meta.json`, a bounded tile cache), re-renders on Eric's Mac over one evening with warm caches, commits `rendered/` by path, and writes the storage budget, size report and yearly checklist. Needs Phases 6 (render staging folder, caches out of iCloud) and 8 (publish by path, tidy) and the page phases landed, so one round carries everything: maps-01 part 3 (contour labels in both units), visual-01 step 2 (the 400x267 card tile) and copy-16's region merges (a site render). Three sittings: a session before the run (items 1-10), the evening of the run (11), a session after it (12-15).

### Prompt

```
PHASE 15: Renderer round and storage budget.

Goal: the safety net, then one re-render of everything, then the budget. This is the only phase that runs tools/render.py; it runs it on Eric's Mac, with his agreement in this session, never in CI.

Decisions to ask Eric at the start (one message):
(a) The hillshade WebP quality (item 8): q80 if the side-by-side at 1x and 2x passes, else keep 88; decide only from the preview the session builds.
(b) The evening for the run (item 11): one evening after Phases 10-14 landed, warm caches, --jobs 2; then once a year. Confirm the disk has room (Phase 6's cache move done; doctor.py reports well over 20 GB free).

=== Before the run ===

1. gap9-01. An offline golden test for the renderer.
   Observation: the tests touch mapkit only through MAP_CSS, Track and line_links (tests/test_build_site.py:608, tests/test_gpxroute.py:600-610, tests/test_region_days.py:587-613), and tests/test_render_goals.py stubs render_map, fetch_osm, render_single and optimize_images (docstring, lines 1-7); tests/ holds GPX fixtures but no DEM tile or Overpass answer. A probe written for the review (scratchpad/finder-gap9/probe.py) replaced geo.get_tile with a synthetic 2 km hill and passed a five-element Overpass dict as spec['osm_data'], and render_map (map-wide, map-col, map-phone), render_profile, render_tile, render_glyph and render_sparkline all ran offline: 16.3 s on Python 3.9.6, 9.7 s on 3.12.11; the 21 output files were byte-identical across PYTHONHASHSEED 0, 4242 and 7 and across both Pythons (mapkit imports no time, datetime or random; its sets are built for membership tests and never iterated; cwebp is optional, render.py:50-53).
   Proposal: tests/render_fixtures.py (a ground(lat, lon) hill around the invented spot 38.9 N 120.1 W of tests/gpx_fixtures.py, a synth_tile(z, x, y) that fills a 256x256 Terrarium-style list from it, and one hand-written Overpass dict with a named lake, a primary road with a ref, a path, a named peak and a village) and tests/test_mapkit_golden.py with golden files under tests/golden/render/<case>/. Three cases: a hike loop (render_map map-wide, map-col, map-phone; render_profile; render_tile; render_glyph; render_sparkline), an mtb out-and-back (is_out_and_back, outbound_only, chevrons) and a two-segment planned route (gpxroute.as_tracks of gf.MULTISEG). Build the specs from render.REPORT and render.SMALL exactly as render_single does (render.py:236-243), with mock.patch.object(geo, 'get_tile', synth_tile) and mapkit.FRAG = mapkit.MAPPNG = a temp dir, restored in cleanup. Hillshade only on the hike loop's map-phone and hillshade=False everywhere else (mapkit.py:782-800 honors it), so the module adds about 10 s on 3.9. Compare the .svg.html and .base.svg text exactly, the per-map .meta.json with floats rounded to 2 dp, and for the PNG only width, height and a sha1 of png_read()'s pixel bytes (never the WebP). UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 rewrites the goldens; a failure prints a unified diff of the SVG split at '>'. If the first CI run on Ubuntu differs from the Mac goldens in a last digit, compare path numbers with a 0.11 px tolerance instead of exact text. Document the command under 'Checking your work' (DEVELOPING.md:1242) with the rule that a renderer change ships with regenerated goldens and a reviewed diff. Fixture coordinates are synthetic: no real trip.
   Files: tests/test_mapkit_golden.py, tests/render_fixtures.py, tests/golden/render/, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

2. gap9-09. A CI-gated contract test: render.py's output is what build.py and core.py read.
   Observation: the page modules read fixed render names from meta.json and the render folder: map-wide/map-col/map-phone (pages/report.py:395, 564, 614), overview-wide/col/phone and day-NN-col/phone, day-NN-profile-col/phone, spark-NN (pages/multiday.py:308-309, 354, 493, 631, 636, 732), overview-tall/overview-phone (pages/series.py:296), profile-* and speed-col (pages/section.py:166-174, 220; core.py:587-592), and tile/g112/g64 (core.py:610-633, build.py:180-187); render.py writes them (render.py:251, 275-287, 304-311, 347-354, 365-375, 379-380, 402-405, 420, and render_site's region-/ski-<slug>-desktop/phone at 633-640, 680). No test runs render.py's output through those readers (tests/test_build_site.py:255-313 and tests/test_report_page.py:294-299 hand-write the fixed names), so a rename in render.py passes every test and the built site then drops the map silently (map_block skips a missing meta key, core.py:500-501) or leaves a '<!-- missing … -->' comment (core.frag, :450-453); sitecheck never scans for it. docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md section 4 (line 247) records that the renderer was never run. render.set_out rewrites mapkit.FRAG/MAPPNG globals (render.py:67-70); render_trip deletes every per-map .meta.json before writing meta.json (:472-474), so base_gz_kb is not in the trip's meta.json; MIN_REGION_TRIPS is 3 (render.py:34); helpers.make_series (tests/helpers.py:103) gives series days dates.
   Proposal: tests/test_render_contract.py, gated with unittest.skipUnless(os.environ.get('CI') or os.environ.get('RENDER_TESTS'), …) so it runs on GitHub Actions (CI=true; note that in the pages.yml comment or set env: CI: 'true') and locally on request. Step 1, a spike: confirm render.render_trip and render.render_site run end to end with only geo.get_tile and render.fetch_osm patched and render.OUT plus each trip's t['rendered'] in a temp dir (optimize_images is a no-op without cwebp, so accept .png or .webp in meta['png']). Step 2: the fixture from item 1. Step 3: render a synthetic single-day trip, a two-day trip (helpers.make_trip(days=[…]) with transfer_before on day 2 and a gap between the day tracks) and a three-day series, and render_site on a region of three trips with one journal part (builders at tests/test_region_days.py:288-325); assert per folder the exact file set and the meta.json keys core reads; then, with core.ROOT and content.RENDERED patched to the temp dir as tests/test_region_page.py:215 and tests/test_archive_page.py:82 do, build the report, multi-day, series, region and map pages and assert every page of a trip with a track has a <div class="mv and no '<!-- missing', and that build.copy_trip_assets publishes exactly the .png-or-.webp, .base.svg and the three drawings. Drop the base_gz_kb assertion (a synthetic DEM says nothing about real budgets). Expected about 40-50 s on CI's Python 3.12; document the gate and RENDER_TESTS=1 in DEVELOPING.md 'Checking your work' and change the 'no test skipped' sentence at :1289-1290; run it once on 3.9 with -W error before merging.
   Files: tests/test_render_contract.py, tests/render_fixtures.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md, .github/workflows/pages.yml (comment or env only).

3. gap9-08. Cap the in-memory tile cache: array storage and an LRU.
   Observation: every decoded terrain tile stays in geo._tile_cache for the life of the process as a 65,536-element list of Python floats (geo.py:179, 213-217): 2.10 MB per tile as a list against 0.53 MB as array('d') (tracemalloc). The on-disk cache holds 2,524 tiles, so a full serial re-render that touches them all holds about 5 GB (arithmetic, not an observed peak), and --jobs keeps that many long-lived forked workers (ProcessPoolExecutor, render.py:741-746) on a 16 GB Mac. Nothing evicts.
   Proposal: in geo.get_tile build array.array('d') ('d', never 'f') instead of a list; DEM.sample's indexing (geo.py:225-235) is unchanged. Keep _tile_cache as an OrderedDict LRU of 256 tiles (about 135 MB as 'd') and move the oldest out when full (256 covers a 1440 px frame at any zoom with margin). Add a case to the golden test: a DEM.sample through a stubbed tile gives the same value from an array as from a list, and the hillshade PNG bytes are unchanged.
   Files: tools/lib/geo.py, tests/test_mapkit_golden.py.

4. gap9-05. Record renderer, Python and cwebp versions in meta.json; warn on a mixed render; stop without cwebp.
   Observation: rendered/trips/<slug>/meta.json holds an opaque hash that folds VERSION in (render.py:430-434) plus maps, charts, start, end and bbox; nothing records which VERSION, Python or cwebp drew a folder, so after a VERSION bump a half-finished re-render that reaches main is invisible to the validator (checks.py:832-834 stops at a missing meta.json; Phase 6's stale-hash warning catches input changes, not a version mix). Today all 168 trip folders have a non-null hash. optimize_images keeps PNGs silently when cwebp is missing (render.py:50-53), so a render on a Mac without cwebp would commit palette PNGs (92 KB for the probe's map-wide against 54 KB for a comparable WebP). mapkit computes base_gz_kb per map (mapkit.py:1739) and render.py drops it at :251.
   Proposal: write meta['renderer'] = {'version': VERSION, 'python': platform.python_version(), 'cwebp': the first line of cwebp -version or null, 'date': today, 'osm': 'cached' | 'fetched' | 'missing'} in both render_trip and render_site (Phase 6's fetched dates feed 'osm'), and keep base_gz_kb per map in meta['maps'] for item 5's sheet. In checks.py add one site-wide warning when the trip folders disagree on renderer.version, or the site meta's version is older than the newest trip's: 'N of 168 renders are at renderer version 7 and M at 8: the re-render is unfinished; run python3 tools/render.py' naming the first five slugs; a folder without a renderer record counts as the oldest version. Make render.py stop before drawing when cwebp is missing unless --allow-png. The warning stays a warning. Tests in tests/test_checks_site_maps.py and tests/test_render_goals.py. The version strings reveal nothing about trips.
   Files: tools/render.py, tools/lib/checks.py, tests/test_checks_site_maps.py, tests/test_render_goals.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

5. gap9-02. A before/after contact sheet of a fixed map sample.
   Observation: a map on the site is three files assembled by core.map_block (hillshade webp img, base.svg img, overlay .svg.html inlined; core.py:484-530), so git diff cannot show what a re-render changed; the two commits that rewrote most of rendered/ touched 2,604 and 2,831 files under it (git diff-tree -r 6abc634 -- rendered; a9baab9), and the three journal-overlay renders of 10-12/13 were checked by comparing pictures side by side in Chrome by hand (docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md:1004-1006). This phase re-renders 168 trips and 24 site maps in one go. map_block reads rendered/site/meta.json through ROOT when site_maps=True (core.py:492-493) and its <img src> is site-relative via core.link, so the sheet must copy or symlink each version's PNG/WebP and base.svg under its own asset folder; core.frag, core.read_json and build.conflict_copy exist to reuse.
   Proposal: tools/render_sheet.py [--base HEAD] [--all] [SLUG …] --out .cache/render-sheet/: one HTML page with a row per sample and two columns, before (the files of git show <base>:rendered/…, binaries through git cat-file -p, extracted into the out folder) and after (the working copy), each map stacked exactly as core.map_block stacks it (reuse core.frag and map_block with a temporary t['rendered'] and an optional base-folder argument on map_block, default unchanged), for map-wide, map-col and map-phone, then profile-wide, tile and g112. Fixed sample: the newest trip of each activity, matterhorn-peak-west-couloir, gran-paradiso-haute-route overview-wide and day-01-col, pacific-crest-trail-2024 overview-tall, region-lake-tahoe-desktop and ski-lake-tahoe-desktop, plus any slug given; --all lists every changed trip as a thumbnail row. Under each pair print bytes before and after, gzip KB of base.svg against BASE_GZ_KB_PHONE / BASE_GZ_KB (40 / 80, mapkit.py:501) and 'unchanged' when the sha1 is equal; a header line counts changed, unchanged, new and removed files under rendered/ from git status --porcelain rendered, skipping conflict copies. No network, stdlib plus git; ends with open of the page. Add the step to ADDING-TRIPS.md '## 5. Publish' (line 186): after a render with no trip name, look at the sheet before git add rendered.
   Files: tools/render_sheet.py, tools/sitegen/core.py, ADDING-TRIPS.md, docs/DEVELOPING.md.

6. gap9-04 and maps-16's --dry-run. A resumable, budgeted run with progress and a summary.
   Observation: --budget stops starting new trips (render.py:708, 752-754, 760) but --force re-renders every trip regardless of meta.json (:762; pending is every trip when a.force, :733-746), so a second --force --budget run starts again from the first trip; a VERSION bump (:31, folded into every hash at :434 and :638) is what makes a plain render.py --budget resumable, but no document says so (ADDING-TRIPS.md:44 and DEVELOPING.md:781, 812 mention --force only for --site; --budget and --jobs are documented nowhere). A full run costs about a minute per single-day trip (ADDING-TRIPS.md:63-64), 113 s for the journal, 267 s for gran-paradiso-haute-route, and 12 to 328 s per site-map job (.cache/render_rest.log), so the 157 single-folder trips, 10 multi-day trips, the journal and 24 site maps are about 3.5 to 4 hours serial on Python 3.9; the probe ran 40 percent faster under python3.12 with byte-identical output. With --budget the run returns before render_site when trips remain (pool :752-754 and serial :759-761), so a resumed job must end with one more run for the site maps; the only progress output is one line per finished trip (:480) and 'budget used up; N trips left' (:753). Phase 6 added --status; no --list or --dry-run exists.
   Proposal: (a) document the full re-render in DEVELOPING.md and ADDING-TRIPS.md: bump VERSION in tools/render.py (never --force for a full run), run python3 tools/render.py --jobs 2 --budget 3600 as often as needed until it reports nothing pending (--jobs 4 only after item 3), then python3 tools/render.py --site, then the sheet of item 5, then git add rendered/trips rendered/site by path; note that /opt/homebrew/bin/python3.12 tools/render.py gave byte-identical files 40 percent faster in the probe and that the tools stay 3.9-compatible. (b) When --force is given without slugs, print 'a full re-render is a VERSION bump plus --budget; --force redraws every trip on every run' and continue. (c) Print one progress line every 10 trips (done/pending, mean seconds per trip, estimated remaining) and a final summary: rendered N, skipped M (hash unchanged), failed K (names), OSM missing J, site maps drawn or not. (d) A --list flag (an alias of Phase 6's --status output extended with why: VERSION, track, title, map:, stats) so the cost is known before the run; it fetches nothing. Tests in tests/test_render_goals.py for --list and the summary with the existing stubs.
   Files: tools/render.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md, tests/test_render_goals.py.

7. maps-16's code, maps-01 part 3, visual-01 step 2, maps-03, maps-05, maps-15, maps-06, copy-18, a11y-06's inline stroke: the renderer changes, on one branch, VERSION bumped once.
   maps-16: the wide map's scale bar is capped at 120 px (mapkit.py:1441, cap_px = min(80 if w <= 400 else 120, SCALE_MAX_FRAC * w)), so the 12.8-mile Matterhorn map shows a '0 to 0.5 mi' bar 64 px long in a 1440 px frame; raise cap_px to 200 for w >= 1200 so 1 mi / 2 km bars fit. VERSION (render.py:31) goes from 7 to 8 once, with the measured cost of a round in a comment beside it (item 13).
   maps-01 part 3: mapkit.contours_svg writes each contour label once in feet (mapkit.py:427-437); write each label with the existing u_span helper (mapkit.py:139-141, two <tspan class="u-mi">/<tspan class="u-km"> in one <text>, as the GPS-max label does) so the KM view reads meters, and the mile loop (mapkit.py:1079-1106) adds a kilometer disc set in a u-km group so the KM view gets km markers; rewrite docs/DEVELOPING.md:87-88 ('Mile discs and contour labels stay imperial') and Phase 10's caption note.
   visual-01 step 2: add a card drawing to GLYPH_SIZES (core.py:610) at 400x267 (3:2) (or 400x300 if the 4:3 row slot is preferred; say which), render it in render.py with render_tile strokes tuned for that size (render.py:255, 381, 424), have trip_card (core.py:1269-1271) and the home featured fallback (home.py:114-115) use it, add it to build.py's drawing list at :180 and the non-scaling-stroke exception at :130, and list it at DEVELOPING.md:1219-1224 (about 168 new SVGs).
   maps-03: every single-day report draws map-wide at 1440x640 and map-col at 718x400 whatever the route's shape (render.py:237-243); on Matterhorn Peak the track box is about 6.1 km north-south by 1.0 km east-west, a thin vertical line in a 2.25:1 frame; 34 of the 157 map-wide renders have a track box taller than 1.4:1 (15 taller than 2.25:1). In render.render_single choose the wide and col frames from the track box's ground aspect (height/width): up to 1.4 keeps 1440x640 / 718x400; above 1.4 gets 1440x800 / 718x500; no third tier (a report map should not exceed about 56 % of a 1440x900 viewport). Add the chosen frame to trip_inputs() only when it is not the default (reading the track's bbox there), so a later frame change re-renders only tall-route trips; phone stays 390x336; multi-day day maps (render.py:355-370) can take the rule later. core.map_block takes w/h from meta.json (core.py:522-523) and .mv has no fixed height (base.css:329-334), so no CSS change. Update DEVELOPING.md:1220 and add a frame-rule test to tests/test_render_goals.py.
   maps-05: the phone overview of a multi-day report (390x260, render.py:348-354: hut_end=None, gpsmax=False, day_labels=False) draws the route, the transfer and the overall start and end only, so a phone reader cannot tell which part of the line is which day, while the desktop overview shows D1-D6, numbered hut squares and the GPS max. Keep hut_end on the overview-phone tracks as {'n': i + 1} without 'label' (mapkit draws the 20 px numbered square and adds the hut name only when 'label' is set, mapkit.py:1029-1048) and leave day_labels off; widen the phone reserve (render.py:351) so squares clear the scale bar and the attribution tag; mapkit already merges squares within 22 px. No key-row change: multiday.drawn() detects the squares from class="mk-day" (multiday.py:91-106). Tests in tests/test_report_page.py.
   maps-15: only the Ski page has a region map panel; render.py:635-637 adds a ski-<region> job for regions with at least two ski trips (506x680 and 390x480); the Mountain biking page (61 rides) and Hiking (34) have none. Generalize the job to <act>-<region> where a region has at least 5 trips of that activity (ski keeps its rule of 2): rides get Santa Cruz (43) and Lake Tahoe (9), hikes get Lake Tahoe alone (no tab row for one region); three new pairs, about 0.6 MB in rendered/site. section.ski_panel (section.py:359-382) becomes section.activity_panel(act, ts) with a per-activity map id, and the Mountain biking and Hiking branches of section.page (:482-537) place it where Ski does, giving their list wrapper data-map-target="<that id>" as section.py:402 does for ski-map (rows already carry data-key, which base.js:157 resolves); checks._site_map_findings (checks.py:987, reading region-* entries only at :1039) and the DEVELOPING.md names list (:1230-1231) take the new keys; extend tests/test_section_page.py (Phase 13's visual-11 creates it) and the fixture keys in tests/test_checks_site_maps.py:48-50.
   maps-06: the journal's overview map (560x860 and 390x600) carries exactly two date labels, 'APR 22' and 'SEP 21' (render.py:410-416 overall_labels; mapkit.py:1476-1488), and the By-month rows highlight their month's days only on hover or focus, so the 3,167-mile line shows no order or season between its ends on a phone. render.render_series passes point_labels=[(lat, lon, 'MAY'), …] for the first recorded day of each month after the first (content dates, nothing invented); mapkit.render_map gains a small point-label layer next to the overall labels (class mk-lbl text with the paper halo, a 6 px leader tick, placed with the existing collision boxes and skipped when nothing fits); both overview renders get the set, the phone one at 12 px; optionally place each label inside the month's <g class="mk-cat" data-key="m-YYYY-MM"> group so base.js highlight() lights it with its tracks. A mapkit unit test covers the placement; tests/test_render_goals.py checks the labels render_series passes.
   copy-18: mapkit.py:1539 writes a cluster's <title> as every member title joined by '; ' (22 titles on one home Lake Tahoe cluster); set it to '%d reports start here: %s' with up to three distinct titles and ' and N more' (Phase 9's gap8-04 rewrites that title at build time for the live pages, so update that rewrite to the same form; a planned route counts as a report in the number, known 15.4.7).
   a11y-06: move the transparent hit stroke on <use class="mk-hit"> (mapkit.py:641-644) from an inline style into a class rule so Phase 12's focus halo works without !important; remove the !important then.
   Then: run tests/test_mapkit_golden.py with UPDATE_GOLDEN=1, review the diff of the goldens against the previous ones (the point of the test), and commit the code with the goldens before the run.

8. perf-05. The hillshade quality, from a side-by-side.
   Observation: render.py converts every hillshade PNG with cwebp -q 88 -m 5 (render.py:50-64). Decoding three shipped rasters and re-encoding them at q75 roughly halves them: region-lake-tahoe-desktop 81,848 → 42,986 bytes, matterhorn map-wide 82,438 → 44,502, map-phone 36,982 → 20,082 (q80: 58,514 / 57,406 / 26,120). The report measured the region map as the largest element on the region pages and the Map page (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md:607); rendered/ holds 595 map rasters, 23.9 MB; a quality change re-encodes them all and adds about 12-14 MB of new binaries to history (WebP blobs never delta-compress), which is why it rides in this one round and not alone.
   Proposal: make the quality a constant HILLSHADE_Q at the top of render.py (default 88 until decided) and raise -m 5 to -m 6; render two or three site maps at q75, q80 and q88 into a scratch folder with --site targeted at a temp output and show Eric the three versions side by side at 1x and 2x; if q75 or q80 passes, set the constant before the run (the VERSION bump already re-renders everything; the rasters change bytes only if the quality changed). Expected per page: about 35-40 KB less for the desktop map and 15 KB for the phone map.
   Files: tools/render.py.

9. The preview sheet before the run: the q75/q80/q88 side-by-side (item 8); the 1000-1199 and 1440 report frames for a tall route (maps-03) and the phone overview squares (maps-05) and the card tile (visual-01) on the contact sheet of the fixed sample, drawn from a scratch render of the sample slugs into a temp output (render.py SLUG --force into a temp OUT, never into rendered/ before the run). Show them to Eric and record his answers.

10. Pre-run checks: both Pythons with -W error including the new golden test and RENDER_TESTS=1 for the contract test; python3 tools/doctor.py (free disk well above 20 GB, no stray index copies, tree clean); python3 tools/render.py --list shows every trip pending with reason VERSION; git count-objects -vH recorded; the caches are in ~/Library/Caches (Phase 6); the before captures of the Matterhorn report, the Haute Route and map/ at 390 and 1280 px are in .cache/plan2/phase15/.

=== The run (Eric's evening, with the internet) ===

11. python3 tools/render.py --jobs 2 --budget 3600 (python3.12 if he prefers; same files), repeated until it reports nothing pending, resuming through the hash and never through --force; then python3 tools/render.py --site; then python3 tools/render_sheet.py --all. During the run: the progress line every 10 trips and the end summary (rendered, skipped, failed, OSM missing, site maps) are kept for the commit message; the Overpass timeout stays 160 s (geo.py:505); a failed trip keeps its old folder (Phase 6) and is re-run after the others. Nothing is staged until item 12.

=== After the run ===

12. Verification and the commit.
   - grep for '"hash": null' and 'osm_failed' under rendered/: none; checks.py's mixed-version warning: silent; python3 tools/render.py --status: nothing pending.
   - git diff --stat rendered/ shows .svg.html, .base.svg and meta.json changes, and .webp changes only if the quality changed; no .png left behind; no .tmp-* folder.
   - The contact sheet in .cache/render-sheet/ reviewed by Eric: tall-route trips (34) show 1440x800 and 718x500 frames; the 10 multi-day phone overviews show numbered day squares; the PCT overview carries month labels; the Matterhorn wide map's scale bar reads 1 mi; contour labels carry u-mi/u-km tspans and the mile loop a km disc set (check the KM view in a browser); cluster tooltips read 'N reports start here: …'; the 400x267 card tile is published and the cards use it; the ski panels exist for Mountain biking (Santa Cruz, Lake Tahoe) and Hiking (Lake Tahoe) with the data-map-target wiring; the region merges' pages (Phase 9) build with no DUPLICATE IDS and the old region URLs with pages still answer.
   - python3 tools/build.py --out <scratch> --link --check passes; both Pythons with -W error pass (the goldens unchanged by the run, since the run is the code the goldens were made from).
   - Stage by path: git add rendered/trips rendered/site (and the assets/maps copies if any are tracked); git status --short shows no ' 2'/' 3'/' 4' names; one commit 'Render round 2026' carrying the printed summary; git count-objects -vH before and after recorded in the log.

13. gap10-01, gap10-03, gap10-04. The storage budget, the cost beside VERSION, the derived-file rule.
   Observation: nothing in the repository states what the site and its history weigh or how fast they grow, although every re-render and every photo copy is committed for good (README.md:6-10). Measured 2026-10-13 at 1cb9cf2, decimal MB from byte counts: the tracked tree 231.9 MB in 5,212 files (photos 144.5 MB in 1,004 files: 398 full copies 108.9 MB, 398 .sm 30.2 MB, 208 .th 5.4 MB; GPX 31.4 MB in 341 files; rendered/ 51.0 MB in 3,393 files: hillshade WebP 23.9, .base.svg 16.0, .svg.html 10.8, meta 0.3); history 303.2 MB of blobs in 9,168 objects, stored as a 182.75 MiB pack plus 36.42 MiB of loose objects, 204.7 MB after a repack; _site/ 240 MB in 3,414 files. Photos and WebP never delta-compress (143.3 MB and 24.9 MB in the pack, zero deltas); SVG and GPX shrink to about 40 % and 17 %. The 58 reports dated 2026 (9 Jan to 24 Sep; the pace divides by the 286 days from 1 Jan to 13 Oct) added 161 photos (57.5 MB of copies), 70 GPX files (3.3 MB) and 1,108 rendered files (14.1 MB): about 95 MB of tracked bytes a year, roughly 86 MB of pack and 103 MB of published site; a single-day trip's render is a median 241 KB in 16 files; each photo about 274 + 76 + 26 KB. GitHub's documented limits (the pages read 2026-10-13, saved by the review): a published Pages site no larger than 1 GB, deploy-pages timeout 10 minutes, soft bandwidth 100 GB a month, source repository recommended 1 GB; a file over 50 MiB warns and over 100 MiB is blocked (Git LFS only; neither page says whether Pages serves LFS files, so LFS is not assumed to be a way out); the whole deploy workflow ran 151-188 s on its five successful runs since 2026-10-12. At the 2026 pace the published site reaches 1 GB in about 7 years and the pack 1 GB in 7-9 years: no pressure today; the point is to keep it that way. The one full round on record, a9baab9 on 2026-09-28, rewrote 2,828 rendered files and cost 9.7 MB of packed history, because only 36 of its 595 hillshade WebPs changed bytes; a round that changes the raster output costs about 24.9 MB more. Git stores blobs by content, so a rewritten file whose bytes are identical adds nothing. docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md:79 already gives the history's file counts; section 4 (line 117) records that no history was rewritten and section 6 leaves a rewrite as decision 6 for Eric.
   Proposal: '## Storage budget' in docs/DEVELOPING.md after 'Measuring page loads', dated, in one unit (decimal MB) with the converting command beside each number (git ls-tree -r -l HEAD summed by kind; git count-objects -vH with MiB converted; du -sh _site), updated with this round's figures: the table; the per-trip costs; GitHub's limits with the docs URL and the date read, and the trailing-year horizons stated as a guard rather than an alarm; three rules for a session: do not re-encode existing photos or hillshades tree-wide for a saving under 30 % of their tracked size (old blobs stay in the pack, new rasters never delta); batch renderer changes so a forced render round happens as rarely as possible, with its measured cost beside render.py's VERSION, and a second forced round in the same year needs a stated reason; anything build.py can derive with the standard library (combined GPX, bundles, indexes, standalone glyph SVGs) is never committed; a phase that adds more than 25 MB to tracked files says so in its log entry with before/after numbers from item 14's tool. A two-row rule table: committed derivatives (need cwebp, sips or the network: photo copies, share crops, route share images, renders) versus build-time derivatives (standard library; written by build.py, never committed, checked by sitecheck.gpx the way the combined multi-day file is, sitecheck.py:664), with the caps the plan attached to the derivative findings written beside them: a 1200 px photo copy only for cover and lead photos (about 16 MB) and only after a 3x-phone measurement shows the 800 px copy visibly short (docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 12.1); share rasters, if Phase 14 built them, one per trip at 1200x630, EXIF stripped, under 150 KB each and 20 MB in all; GPX bundles, if ever, written by build.py and offered only where a region's bundle is under 6 MB (Santa Cruz at 8.9 MB keeps per-trip files only; Pages serves GPX uncompressed, so a bundle downloads at full size); a .th copy for the 190 photos without one is fine at about 5 MB. A comment beside VERSION at render.py:31: 'a bump re-renders all 3,393 files; git keeps only the ones whose bytes change: 9.7 MB of packed history when only the SVG changes (a9baab9, 2026-09-28), about 35 MB when the hillshade rasters change too; after a round, git diff --stat -- rendered | tail -1 is the cost: put it in the commit message', updated with this round's measured figure. One pointer line under README.md:114; cross-link docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md:79.
   Files: docs/DEVELOPING.md, README.md, tools/render.py (one comment).

14. gap10-02. tools/size_report.py.
   Observation: no tool in tools/ measures size (grep for size_report, storage budget, git gc or count-objects over tools, tests, docs, README.md and ADDING-TRIPS.md matches nothing); the numbers above took a dozen hand-written git and Python commands, and the obvious proxy for a VERSION bump (the tracked size of rendered/, 51.0 MB) is five times the measured history cost of the one round on record (9.7 MB). content.load_all already sets a multi-day or series trip's date to its first day's (content.py:586); the bulk import landed on one day, so grouping is by trip date, not commit date.
   Proposal: tools/size_report.py (stdlib, read-only, no git write, 3.9-compatible, under about 150 lines): 'tracked' from git ls-tree -r -l HEAD grouped by kind (photos full/.sm/.th by the suffixes of tools/lib/photos.py; GPX; rendered by .webp/.png, .base.svg, .svg.html, meta.json; everything else); 'history' from git count-objects -v only (no verify-pack); 'site' by walking _site/ or --site DIR by the same kinds; 'growth' by loading content.load_all(), taking each trip's date, and summing its photos, GPX and rendered/trips/<slug> bytes per calendar year, printed as a table with the LIMITS (values, docs URL, date read) and 'at the last full year's pace: site 1 GB in N.N years, pack 1 GB in N.N years', plus one line saying the grouping is by trip date. Skip conflict copies with sitecheck.conflict_copy. --json prints one object. Tests in tests/test_size_report.py on synthetic trips in a temp folder: the kind classifier, the per-year grouping, the horizon arithmetic; the git parts run only if git and a repository are present. Document it in 'Checking your work' and the file table, and as the first item of item 15's checklist.
   Files: tools/size_report.py, tests/test_size_report.py, docs/DEVELOPING.md, README.md.

15. gap10-06. A yearly upkeep checklist with this year's values.
   Observation: the deploy depends on things that change without a commit: five GitHub Actions pinned by major tag (checkout@v4, setup-python@v5, configure-pages@v5, upload-pages-artifact@v3, deploy-pages@v4, pages.yml:24-45) and, after Phase 8, setup-node@v4; the ubuntu-latest image (24.04.5, image 20261004.327.1 today, Node 22.23.3, Python 3.12.3 on the image and 3.12.15 installed by setup-python); Python '3.12' (pages.yml:27; CPython 3.12 leaves support in October 2028); GitHub's Pages limits; the fonts (Google's URL at core.py:32-33, or the self-hosted files after Phase 14); the OSM Overpass endpoints and the AWS terrain bucket (geo.py:178, :458); the attribution the map data licenses require (footer core.py:1698, captions :720, the in-map tag :521; the caption credits are tested at tests/test_report_page.py:82 and :1282, the footer and the tag are not). The first deploy failed on runner drift (docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md:945-951). OSM answers in the cache never expire (geo.py:462-464) and no flag refreshes them, so a forced render alone redraws from last year's data. The validator reports a conflict copy under content/ as an error (checks.py:571). ADDING-TRIPS.md's last section is 'If something goes wrong' (now 'When something goes wrong').
   Proposal: '## Yearly upkeep' in docs/DEVELOPING.md after the Storage budget, a dated checklist with this year's value beside each item and one line 'if it changed, do this', plus a pointer under README.md:114 and a sentence at the end of ADDING-TRIPS.md's recovery section tying it to the first trip of each year. Items: (1) size: tools/size_report.py and git count-objects -vH; git gc when loose objects exceed 1,000; (2) workflow: for each uses: in pages.yml note the newest major and any deprecation notice; bump in one commit and prove it with workflow_dispatch on a quiet day; (3) runner: read the ubuntu-latest image README and run the Node harnesses on the shipped Node; plan the pages.yml Python bump before October 2028 and keep 3.9 while the Mac's system Python is 3.9.6; (4) limits: re-read the Pages limits page and update the budget table; (5) credits and endpoints: add one assertion for the footer strings at core.py:1698 and the in-map tag at :521 to an existing test file; confirm the terrain bucket, both Overpass endpoints and the font source still answer; (6) map data: delete .cache/osm (now under ~/Library/Caches) before the year's single forced render round so the region maps pick up a year of OSM edits, never as a separate --site --force; (7) iCloud: look for conflict names under content/ (an error that stops the build), rendered/ and assets/ (git-ignored) and .git/index N, with tools/tidy.py and tools/doctor.py; (8) run tools/validate.py and tools/privacy_audit.py and read the summaries.
   Files: docs/DEVELOPING.md, README.md, ADDING-TRIPS.md, one existing test file.

16. Only if Eric publishes Phase 15 without the review gate (otherwise the gate's step 6 is the push): publish through Phase 8's publish.py (the render commit plus the docs commit) only when Eric says so, then compare the live Matterhorn, Haute Route and map/ pages with the before captures at 390 and 1280 px and record the differences.

What not to touch: content/ (the merges were Phase 9's); the committed rendered/ tree before the run (every pre-run render goes to a temp OUT); git history; the hash inputs except the frame rule and the one VERSION bump; CI never runs render.py.

Acceptance gate (run all of it and put the numbers in the report):
- Before the bump: tests/test_mapkit_golden.py (hike loop, mtb out-and-back, two-segment planned route, about 10 s) and tests/test_render_contract.py (CI-gated, self-contained fixture, about 50 s) pass on 3.9 and 3.12 with -W error and the goldens are byte-identical across two PYTHONHASHSEED values; a DEM.sample through an array('d') tile equals the list result and the hillshade PNG bytes are unchanged; render_trip and render_site write meta['renderer'] (version, python, cwebp, date, osm) and the validator warns on a fixture whose folders disagree on the version; render.py stops without cwebp unless --allow-png.
- Preview first: two or three site maps rendered at q75, q80 and q88 into a scratch folder and shown to Eric at 1x and 2x; the 1000-1199 and 1440 report frames, the phone overview squares and the card tile shown on the contact sheet of a fixed sample before the full run; if the quality changes, HILLSHADE_Q is set before the run.
- The run: VERSION bumped once; --list/--status lists every trip; python3 tools/render.py --jobs 2 --budget 3600 prints a progress line every 10 trips and an end summary (rendered, skipped, failed, OSM missing, site maps) and resumes across evenings through the hash, never through --force; the OSM answers used carry their 'fetched' date; the Overpass timeout stays 160 s.
- After the run: no rendered/**/meta.json has hash null or osm_failed (grep); git diff --stat rendered/ shows .svg.html, .base.svg and meta.json only (and .webp only if the quality changed); the contact sheet in .cache/render-sheet/ reviewed by Eric; tall-route trips (34 with a track box over 1.4:1) show 1440x800 and 718x500 frames; the 10 multi-day phone overviews show numbered day squares; ski panels exist for Mountain biking (Santa Cruz, Lake Tahoe) and Hiking (Lake Tahoe) with the data-map-target wiring; the PCT overview carries month labels; cluster tooltips read 'N reports start here: …'; the Matterhorn wide map's scale bar reads 1 mi; contour labels carry u-mi/u-km tspans and the mile loop a km disc set; a 400x267 card tile is published and the cards use it; the region merges' pages build with no DUPLICATE IDS and the old region URLs with pages still answer.
- Staged with git add by path (rendered/trips, rendered/site), git status shows no ' 2'/' 3' names, one commit 'Render round 2026' carrying the printed summary; git count-objects -vH before and after recorded; DEVELOPING.md's 'Storage budget' section holds the dated table in decimal MB with the commands beside each number, the per-trip costs, GitHub's limits with the URL and date, the committed-versus-build-time rule table and the caps attached to the derivative findings; tools/size_report.py (stdlib, read-only) reproduces those numbers and tests/test_size_report.py covers the classifier and per-year grouping; the 'Yearly upkeep' checklist exists with this year's values (actions, runner, limits, credits test, delete .cache/osm before the next round) and a comment beside VERSION states the measured cost of a round (9.7 MB packed for an SVG-only round, about 35 MB when the hillshades change, and this round's figure).
- If published from this phase without the gate: publish through Phase 8's publish.py and compare the live Matterhorn, Haute Route and map/ pages with the before captures; both Pythons and build.py --check pass; render.py is never run in CI.

Screenshots: the three preview sheets, the contact sheet (kept in .cache/render-sheet/), the before/after captures of the Matterhorn report, the Haute Route and map/ at 390 and 1280 px in .cache/plan2/phase15/, and the KM view of a report map showing meter contour labels and km discs.

Log entry: 'Phase 15 - Renderer round and storage budget' (three sittings in one entry, or one entry per sitting). Under author inputs: the quality Eric chose, the evening, the measured cost of the round, the count-objects figures, the pending yearly items.
```

**Gate:** the golden and contract tests pass on both Pythons before the bump and the goldens match across two hash seeds; the quality and the frames were chosen from the preview sheets; the run ended with 'nothing pending', no `hash null` or `osm_failed` remains, and `git diff --stat rendered/` shows only SVG and meta changes (plus WebP only if the quality changed); the contact sheet was reviewed and the eleven after-run checks above hold in the build; one 'Render round 2026' commit staged by path with the summary in its message and the count-objects figures in the log; the Storage budget, `size_report.py` and the Yearly upkeep checklist exist with this round's numbers; the live pages match the build after the publish.

## Review gate before each publication

Runs before each publication: after Phase 15 if he does them all, or after whichever earlier phase he publishes from (once per push). Modeled on the 2026-10-11 gate (`docs/WEBSITE_IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md` from line 788; `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md` section 15): the whole run is compared with the site that is public today, what a push makes public is listed, and the push happens only on Eric's word in the session.

### Prompt

```
REVIEW GATE BEFORE PUBLICATION.

Goal: compare everything built since the last push with the site that is public today, prove that nothing a visitor relies on was lost, list what a push makes public, and push only when Eric says so in this session. Nothing under content/ changes in this session (except by Eric's explicit request), tools/render.py is not run, and no history is rewritten. Run each tool and each replay only where its phase has landed (check.py, doctor.py, tidy.py and smoke.py from Phase 8; share_preview.py from Phase 14; size_report.py from Phase 15; docs/CHECKING_WITH_A_SCREEN_READER.md from Phase 13C; the Filters button and zero-result buttons from Phase 7; Continue reading from Phase 11; the cluster selection, legend toggles and coarse-pointer taps from Phase 12; the profile readout from Phase 10) and list the rest under not applicable with the phase that adds it. The Lighthouse target is 100 on the Matterhorn report and no score lower than today's on any page (home accessibility 96 until Phase 13C's a11y-03).

Starting point to record first: git log --oneline origin/main..HEAD (the commits since the last push, or the uncommitted working tree if phases were not committed), git status --short, the Python versions, the date.

1. Two builds, file by file.
   - Run python3 tools/doctor.py first (from Phase 8; before that, df -h). Make a worktree of the public commit outside iCloud: git worktree add <scratch>/public-worktree origin/main (about 232 MB on the local disk, the one copy of the checkout the standing rules allow; remove it with git worktree remove at the end). If free disk is under 30 GB, make no worktree: build the public commit from git archive origin/main | tar -x -C <scratch>/public-src instead and delete that folder right after the comparison. Build it with its own code: python3 tools/build.py --out <scratch>/public-site --link --check from inside the worktree. Build the working tree with python3 tools/build.py --out <scratch>/new-site --link --check.
   - Confirm the baseline is the live site: curl -s the live home page, trips/, the Matterhorn report, the Haute Route, the PCT page, map/, a region page, the tick list, About and the 404, and compare each with the public-site build (identical bytes, or identical apart from the © year if the year changed).
   - Compatibility: every path of the public site exists in the new build (list the paths that are new and the paths that are gone; a gone path needs a reason and Eric's yes); all 351 GPX files byte-identical; every write-up identical on every page (extract the .prose text of each trip page in both builds and compare); every title in H1 and in the BlogPosting headline unchanged; every canonical, every documented anchor (DEVELOPING.md's id lists; #latest-h; #day-N; #region-<slug>; #yr-YYYY; #y-YYYY; #season-…; #arc-row-<slug>) present; the feed's guids unchanged; the sitemap's addresses a superset of today's (name any removed one).
   - For each page that differs, say in one line why (the phase and item). Put the counts (pages, differing pages, new pages, files) in the log.

2. The checks, actually run, on the final working tree.
   - /usr/bin/python3 -W error -m unittest discover -s tests and python3.12 -W error -m unittest discover -s tests: counts, time, skips (RENDER_TESTS=1 once for the contract test); tests/run_orders.py; the Node harnesses with their check counts; python3 tools/check.py; python3 tools/validate.py (0 errors; the warning count and what they are); python3 tools/build.py --out <scratch>/new-site --link --check (pages, trips, the check lines); python3 tools/doctor.py; python3 tools/tidy.py (listing); python3 tools/share_preview.py (the flag counts); python3 tools/size_report.py; python3 tools/render.py --status (nothing pending); python3 tools/smoke.py against the built site if Chrome is present.
   - CI rehearsal as the 2026-10-11 gate did: the files a commit of this change holds, Python 3.12, TZ=UTC, LANG=C, Node 22 if available, the workflow's commands; say what could not be rehearsed (Linux, the runner's exact patch release).
   - Lighthouse 13 on the built site, served locally under /personal-website/: the 15 pages of the earlier gate (home, trips/, the Matterhorn report, a bare ride, the Haute Route, the PCT, map/, two region pages, the tick list, the goal page, About, three section pages), mobile and desktop: accessibility, best practices and SEO, with the one informational audit noted; the performance category reported but not gated (the review that made this plan took no traces, so these are the first figures since Phase 5: record them).
   - Browser replays in Chrome at 1440, 1024 and 390 px, back/forward cache on and off, with real key presses, clicks and touch: the explorer (search, facets, sorts, Filters button, zero-result buttons, Back), the header search from a report, the 404 search, the tick list, the journal (month tabs, write-ups filter, Continue reading, Back after a reveal), the viewer (open, swipe, Back gesture emulated, error state), the units switch and the km default, the menu (Home, Units), the cluster selection and legend toggles on map/, the coarse-pointer taps on a region page, the full-screen map opening on the route with JavaScript off, the profile readout, the print preview, forced colors and prefers-contrast emulation, site.js blocked. No console errors or page exceptions on the 15 pages at the three widths.
   - Screenshots: the earlier gate's set (39 views of main, 51 of the branch) extended with the 768, 1024 and 1180 px tablet profile from Phase 13, paired on a local review page in .cache/review/; say which views are byte-identical, which changed and why.
   - The real-device and account checks that only Eric can do, each done or pending with the date: the VoiceOver script (docs/CHECKING_WITH_A_SCREEN_READER.md), the real-phone look (Back gesture, swipe, no-JS map), the share preview paste, Search Console, the Rich Results Test, the W3C feed validation.

3. What a push makes public (an inventory for Eric to approve, in the log and in the chat).
   - New files in the repository: every new tool, test, fixture, golden, font and license text, the repository CLAUDE.md, the recovery and setup sections, docs/CHECKING_WITH_A_SCREEN_READER.md, the storage budget.
   - New facts on the site: the email alias and profile links if Eric supplied them, the published_at lines he filled, the places, titles, merges, summits, About body, region introductions and the home region_maps key if he wrote them, the source: lines, the new title tags, descriptions, og and JSON-LD tags, the feed's full write-ups.
   - New behavior: the list from each phase's log entry, one line each.
   - Privacy: run python3 tools/privacy_audit.py and compare its summary with the one before the run; confirm docs/PRIVACY_NOTES.md says what is true after the run (an email is published, if so); confirm no test, fixture, golden or doc carries a real write-up, a real recording, a home location, a start coordinate or a time someone is away; grep the new files for the author's personal export paths and for anything the workspace CLAUDE.md marks as outside the repository.
   - Choices made for the author during the phases (interface copy, labels, the frame, the quality, defaults he did not answer): list them so he can change any before the push.

4. Open points: carry forward every 'Remaining issues and author inputs' item from the Phase 6-15 entries that is still open, and the earlier ones from docs/IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md 10, 12, 14.6, 14.7, 15.4 that this plan did not close (list which it closed, with the phase). Record what was not run (Firefox; anything from 2 above that could not be done).

5. Going back, written before the push: the durable path (git revert of the new commits, then git push; about three minutes to live), the temporary path (gh run rerun of the last good run within 30 days), never a forced push; the fact that history now contains whatever the inventory lists.

6. The push, only on Eric's word.
   - Ask in one message: 'Push to main now? It publishes the inventory above.' Do nothing until he answers yes in this session.
   - First bring main up to the reviewed state with a fast-forward only: git checkout main; git merge --ff-only <phase branch> (one branch at a time, in phase order); if a merge cannot fast-forward, stop and say so instead of merging or rebasing.
   - Then: python3 tools/publish.py --push for the commits (or git push origin main when the commits are already made and publish.py's per-trip flow does not apply), watch the run with gh run watch, and when it is green: curl -I the live versioned stylesheet (200, max-age=600); compare the live Matterhorn report, the Haute Route, map/ and the home with the new-site build (identical bytes); Lighthouse on the live home and Matterhorn report (mobile): accessibility 100 expected on the report, and on the home once Phase 13C has landed (96 before); then the account checks Eric does (Search Console sitemap submission, the Rich Results Test, the W3C feed validator, the share preview paste), each logged.
   - If the run is red: nothing was deployed; read the failed step, fix locally, re-run the two Pythons and --check, push again; say so in the log.

7. Log entry: 'Review gate before publication (<date>)' with the format of the 2026-10-11 entry (the starting point, Changed files, Behaviour, Checks actually run with every count and the build each ran on, what was not run, Review), followed by 'Publication (<date>)' once the push lands, with the run id, the time to live, the live comparison and the after-deploy checks. Then remove the worktree.
```

**Gate:** every path of the public site exists in the new build, all 351 GPX files and every write-up are byte-identical, every H1 title and documented anchor survives; both Pythons pass with `-W error` and the CI rehearsal passes; Lighthouse accessibility, best practices and SEO are 100 on the 15 pages, mobile and desktop; the browser replays show no console error at 1440, 1024 and 390 px; the inventory of what becomes public is in the log and Eric approved it in the session; the rollback is written before the push; the push happened only on his word, the run was watched to green, and the live pages match the build.
